<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that zero-frequency boundary measurements on any nonempty open boundary patch determine both a smooth Riemannian metric and a smooth unitary connection on the trivial Hermitian rank-two bundle over a compact connected smooth manifold of dimension at least three with smooth boundary. Both inputs and observations are restricted to the same patch. The metric and connection are determined up to a diffeomorphism and a unitary gauge that restrict to the identity on the measured patch.

- **claim-joint-same-patch-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $M$ and $\Gamma$ be as above. For $j=1,2$, let $g_j$ be a smooth Riemannian metric on $M$ and let $A_j\in C^\infty(M;T^*M\otimes\mathfrak u(2))$. If $$\langle\Lambda_{g_1,A_1,\Gamma}f,h\rangle =\langle\Lambda_{g_2,A_2,\Gamma}f,h\rangle \quad\text{for all }f,h\in C_c^\infty(\Gamma;\mathbb C^2),$$ then there are a smooth diffeomorphism $\Phi:M\longrightarrow M$ and a smooth map $U:M\longrightarrow U(2)$ such that $$\label{eq:main-conclusion} \Phi\|_\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{Id}},\qquad U\|_\Gamma=I,\qquad g_2=\Phi^*g_1,\qquad A_2=U^{-1}(\Phi^*A_1)U+U^{-1}dU.$$ Here $I$ denotes the $2\times2$ identity matrix.
  - Explanation: Equality of the local boundary energy forms determines the smooth metric and unitary connection up to a diffeomorphism and unitary gauge fixing the measured patch.
- **claim-boundary-metric-connection-jets** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bdy-jets)): Suppose the local energy forms of $(g_1,A_1)$ and $(g_2,A_2)$ agree on $\Gamma$. In their respective boundary-normal coordinates, based on the same boundary coordinates, and in the normal gauges just constructed, the full Taylor jets of $g_1,A_1$ and $g_2,A_2$ agree at every point of $\Gamma$.
  - Explanation: After choosing corresponding boundary-normal coordinates and normal gauges, equality of the boundary forms gives all metric and connection Taylor jets on the measured patch.
- **claim-common-exterior-green-data** (construction, [source](#stmt-bdy-cap)): There are compact connected smooth extensions $N_j$ of the original manifold, with nonempty smooth boundary, and a common connected open exterior cap $E\subset N_j^\circ$, attached through a nonempty open patch $P\Subset\Gamma$, such that the extended metrics and connections agree on $E$. For each $f\in C_c^\infty(E;\mathbb C^2)$, the solutions $L_{j,D}^{-1}f$ agree on $E$. The Dirichlet Green matrices, normalized using metric volume, satisfy $$\label{bdy:common-green} G_1(x,y)=G_2(x,y)\quad(x,y\in E,\ x\ne y), \qquad G_j(x,y)=G_j(y,x)^*.$$
  - Explanation: A common smooth exterior cap converts the boundary measurements into common source solutions and Green matrices on an interior open set.
- **claim-source-values-and-harmonic-jets** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bdy-evaluations)): Let $N$ be a compact connected smooth manifold of dimension $n\ge2$ with nonempty boundary, let $L=d_A^{*g}d_A$, and let $U\Subset N^\circ$ be a nonempty open source set.* 1. *At each interior point, $f\mapsto L_D^{-1}f(p)$ is onto $\mathbb C^2$. At any two distinct interior points the two values are jointly onto $\mathbb C^2\oplus\mathbb C^2$.* 2. *If $p\notin\overline U$, the second jets of $L_D^{-1}f$ at $p$ fill exactly the linear space of jets satisfying $Lu(p)=0$.* 3. *Each evaluation is zero on $\partial N$, and for fixed $f$ varies continuously up to that boundary.
  - Explanation: Source solutions realize arbitrary fiber values, independent values at distinct points, and precisely the harmonic second jets away from the source set. Their boundary evaluations vanish continuously.
- **claim-unique-maximal-match** (construction, [source](#stmt-bdy-matches)): All matches agree on overlaps and are injective on base points. Their union is a unique maximal match $(\Phi,J)$ on a connected open set $W\subset N_2^\circ$. On this set, $$\label{bdy:matched-green} G_1(\Phi(p),\Phi(q)) =J(p)G_2(p,q)J(q)^*\qquad(p,q\in W,\ p\ne q).$$
  - Explanation: Joint source-value surjectivity makes local matches unique and injective. They glue into a maximal match carrying the Green matrices into each other.
- **claim-frontier-contact-data** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bdy-contact)): If the maximal domain $W$ has an interior frontier, there are a point $p\in\partial W\cap N_2^\circ$, a point $q\in N_1^\circ$, and charts about $p,q$, both with coordinate range a neighborhood of $0\in\mathbb R^n$, with the following properties.* 1. *Both charts avoid $\overline{U_0}$. The old match is coordinate identity on its domain in these charts. That domain contains a region $x_n<\kappa(x')$, where $\kappa$ is smooth, $\kappa(0)=0$, and [(bdy:limit-match)](#bdy:limit-match)$d\kappa(0)=0$.* 2. *In smooth local unitary frames, the metrics, connection matrices, and Green matrices agree on the known region (for the Green matrices, on its square off the diagonal), and $g_1(0)=g_2(0)=I_n$.* 3. *There are invertible matrix functions $F_j$ with harmonic columns, chosen as corresponding source solutions. In these harmonic frames there are corresponding harmonic matrix functions $X_j^l$, $1\le l\le n$, satisfying $$\label{bdy:trace-coordinates} \tfrac12\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{tr}X_j^l(x)=x^l, \qquad X_j^l(0)=0, \qquad \partial_iX_j^l(0)=\delta_i^l I.$$* 4. *There are finitely many further corresponding harmonic column functions with zero value and gradient at $0$, whose Hessians at $0$ span the space of symmetric $\mathbb C^2$-valued tensors $H$ satisfying $\sum_i H_{ii}=0$. All these paired functions, including the frames, agree on the known region and have identical jets at $0$.
  - Explanation: A putative interior frontier admits common contact coordinates, unitary frames, harmonic frames, trace-normalized coordinate matrices, and a finite family of corresponding harmonic Hessians.
- **claim-product-extension-from-cross** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cross-series)): Let $D_i$ be bounded connected coordinate domains, and let $S_i\Subset D_i$ be nonempty open sets. Suppose a product solution $F$ is given consistently on $$(D_1\times S_2)\cup(S_1\times D_2),$$ with the sum of its two $L^2$ norms at most $M$. Suppose $O_i\Subset D_i$ are open sets and every $V_i$-valued solution $w$ of $\mathsf L_iw=0$ on $D_i$ satisfies $$\label{cross:interpolation-input} \\|w\\|_{L^2(O_i)} \le C\\|w\\|_{L^2(S_i)}^{\gamma_i} \\|w\\|_{L^2(D_i)}^{1-\gamma_i}, \qquad 0<\gamma_i<1,\qquad \gamma_1+\gamma_2>1.$$ Then $F$ extends as a product solution to $O_1\times O_2$, agreeing with the two given pieces on their intersections. Its $C^m$ norm on every compact subset of the output product is at most $C_mM$. These constants are uniform when geometric margins, ellipticity, the required coefficient bounds, interpolation constants, and a positive lower bound for $\gamma_1+\gamma_2-1$ are uniform. A specified output order requires only finitely many coefficient bounds.
  - Explanation: Compatible product solutions extend from a cross when the factor interpolation exponents have a sum exceeding the summability threshold. The extension has uniform compact-subset smooth bounds under uniform margins and coefficient bounds.
- **claim-strict-product-hessian-extension** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cross-criterion)): Let $\rho$ be smooth near $z_0=(x_0,y_0)$, with $\rho(z_0)=0$ and $d_x\rho,d_y\rho\ne0$. Put $$a_i=\frac{\nabla_i\rho}{\|\nabla_i\rho\|^2},\qquad H=\operatorname{Hess}\rho.$$ Suppose that at $z_0$ $$\label{cross:hessian-test} H((a_1,-a_2),(a_1,-a_2)) +H((v_1,v_2),(v_1,v_2))>0 \quad \left(v_i\perp a_i,\ \|v_i\|=\|a_i\|\right).$$ Every product solution given on $\{\rho>0\}$ near $z_0$ extends to a neighborhood of $z_0$, agreeing on a smaller part of that side. In fact, the construction uses only two compact product sets in $\{\rho>\varepsilon\}$ for some $\varepsilon>0$. The neighborhoods and estimates on smaller neighborhoods are uniform under small smooth perturbations preserving the strict hypotheses, provided the data on those compact sets are bounded.
  - Explanation: Nonvanishing partial gradients and the strict product Hessian inequality permit local continuation across the hypersurface. The construction uses compact data strictly inside the known side.
- **claim-compact-cylinder-propagation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cross-propagation)): Suppose a region in a product patch has smooth coordinates $(z,a)$ near $B\times[a_-,a_+]$, where $B$ is a compact base, possibly with boundary or corners. A single product solution is given on an open set $\mathcal S$ containing neighborhoods of the top and lateral boundary. Assume that $\mathcal S$ is upward closed in $a$ at fixed $z$. If every level $a=\text{constant}$ satisfies Proposition <a href="#cross:criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cross:criterion">8</a> outside $\mathcal S$, with the positive side pointing toward increasing $a$, the solution extends to a neighborhood of the cylinder, agreeing on $\mathcal S$. For fixed coordinates, compact sets and strict reference inequalities, the extension has uniform compact-subset bounds under small smooth perturbations, in terms of bounds on finitely many compact subsets of the initially given region.
  - Explanation: Local continuation germs glue along the compact cylinder because overlap components connect vertically to an already identified region. The upward-closed seed is preserved.
- **claim-fixed-separation-initialization** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cross-initial)): In this setting, let $n\ge3$ and fix $B>0$, $d>0$ and $\eta>0$. For all sufficiently small $\lambda$, the data <a href="#cross:mixed-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cross:mixed-data">[cross:mixed-data]</a> have a product-solution continuation to a neighborhood of $$\{(x,y):\|x\|,\|y\|\le B,\ \|x-y\|\ge d\}.$$ Every fixed $C^m$ norm on a smaller neighborhood is bounded uniformly in $\lambda$. It agrees with $G_{1,\lambda}$ when $y\in W_\lambda$ and with $G_{2,\lambda}$ when $x\in W_\lambda$, on the corresponding overlaps. In particular these agreements hold on the fixed truncated lower strips $y_n<-\eta$ and $x_n<-\eta$, respectively. The smallness threshold for $\lambda$ and the constants may depend on $B,d,\eta,m$.
  - Explanation: After a sufficiently small spatial dilation, the mixed Green matrix continues across all bounded pairs with a fixed positive separation, with uniform smooth bounds and agreement with the original cross.
- **claim-uniform-shrinking-radius-profiles** (bound, [source](#stmt-sph-profiles)): For the geometry just fixed and all sufficiently small $\lambda$, the profiles <a href="#sph:radius" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sph:radius">[sph:radius]</a> satisfy $$\label{sph:profile-bounds} \beta\ge cp_*,\qquad \operatorname{Hess}_\gamma f_*\le-c\beta\gamma, \qquad \|\partial^j f_*\|\le C_j\beta^j\quad(j\ge1),$$ uniformly in $0<\delta\le1$. The constants $c,C_j$ are independent of $p_*\ge2$. For fixed $p_*,R_*$, the functions $r_\delta^{1/p_*}$ have a uniform Lipschitz bound. The radii increase strictly with $\delta$, converge to zero where $t\le0$, and obey $$\label{sph:amplitude-smallness} \sup_{0<\delta\le1,\ y\in Y}r_\delta\beta^2 \longrightarrow0\quad\hbox{as }R_*\downarrow0$$ for each fixed $p_*$.
  - Explanation: The logarithmic radius has uniform concavity and derivative bounds. The radii increase with the regularization parameter, shrink on nonpositive depth, and satisfy amplitude smallness.
- **claim-strict-moving-sphere-geometry** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sph-strict)): Let $r=r_\delta$ satisfy <a href="#sph:profile-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sph:profile-bounds">[sph:profile-bounds]</a>, <a href="#sph:smallness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sph:smallness">[sph:smallness]</a>, and <a href="#sph:radius-smallness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sph:radius-smallness">[sph:radius-smallness]</a>. For $p_*$ sufficiently large and $\varepsilon$ sufficiently small, the hypersurface $$\rho(x,y)=\tfrac12\bigl(d_\gamma(x,y)^2-r(y)^2\bigr)=0$$ satisfies the strict criterion of Proposition <a href="#cross:criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cross:criterion">8</a> for principal metrics $\gamma$ in the first factor and $Q^{-1}$ in the second. Both partial gradients are nonzero, and the positive side is the exterior of the moving ball.
  - Explanation: The logarithmic radius concavity produces a positive remainder after cancellation of the leading radius-gradient terms. The moving sphere therefore satisfies the strict product continuation criterion.
- **claim-green-continuation-to-shrinking-spheres** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sph-continuation)): Make the choices of Remark <a href="#sph:choices" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sph:choices">12</a>. For all sufficiently small $\lambda$ and every $0<\delta\le1$ satisfying <a href="#sph:smallness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sph:smallness">[sph:smallness]</a>, the mixed Green matrix extends to a neighborhood of $$\{(x,y):y\in Y,\ d_\gamma(x,y)=r_\delta(y)\}.$$ It solves the first system in $x$ and the conjugate second system in the second tensor index. On the lower center region $y_n<-1/4$ it agrees with $G_1$, and on the prescribed positive-depth region $t>t_b$ it agrees with the fixed-separation continuation of Proposition <a href="#cross:initial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cross:initial">10</a>. The extension is smooth for each positive $\delta$.
  - Explanation: The mixed Green matrix extends to every admissible positive-radius sphere family. Lower centers retain the original Green matrix, while positive-depth centers retain the fixed-separation continuation.
- **claim-matrix-harmonic-transfer** (construction, [source](#stmt-sph-transfer)): Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href="#sph:continuation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sph:continuation">14</a>, there is a smooth matrix function $\psi=\psi_\delta$ on the unit sphere bundle over $Y^\circ$ such that $$\label{sph:density-representation} T_yv=\int_{S_y}\psi(y,\omega) v\bigl(\exp_y^\gamma(r(y)\omega)\bigr)\,d\sigma_y(\omega).$$ For every $y_0\in Y^\circ$ and every first harmonic-frame function $v$ defined near $\overline{B_\gamma(y_0,r(y_0))}$, the function $y\mapsto T_yv$ is second harmonic-frame harmonic on a neighborhood of $y_0$. For each pair $(v_1,v_2)$ of first and second harmonic-frame functions defined on the fixed coordinate neighborhoods and agreeing on the known side, $$\label{sph:reproduction} \int_{S_y}\psi(y,\omega)\,d\sigma_y(\omega)=I, \qquad T_yv_1=v_2(y).$$
  - Explanation: A smooth matrix sphere density represents the transfer functional. It sends local first-system harmonic inputs to second-system harmonic outputs, reproduces corresponding harmonic pairs, and has identity total mass.
- **claim-cutoff-density-regularity** (bound, [source](#stmt-sph-cutoff-bounds)): After the choices in Remark <a href="#sph:choices" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sph:choices">12</a>, for every fixed integer $m$ the angular $H^m$ norms of $\psi_\delta$ and its first base derivatives on $\operatorname{supp}(d\chi)$ are bounded independently of admissible $\delta$ and sufficiently small $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: The transfer density and its first base derivatives have uniform angular regularity where the fixed cutoff varies. The lower region uses ball evaluation, and the positive-depth region retains a positive radius.
- **claim-moving-trace-expansions** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-trace)): Under <a href="#ang:profile-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:profile-bounds">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>–<a href="#ang:smallness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:smallness">[ang:smallness]</a>, $$\label{ang:C-expansion} \mathcal C_{i,0}^* =r^{-1}\mathsf A_i^*+f_i\mathsf B+E_i+F_i, \qquad E_i\in r\Psi^1,\quad F_i\in\Psi^0.$$ The normalized remainders $r^{-1}E_i,F_i$ have the allowance $C_j\beta^j$ through six base derivatives. Moreover, on rows, $$\label{ang:trace-difference} K_i:=\mathcal C_i^t-\mathcal C_{i,0}^* \in\Psi^0,\qquad \nabla K_i\in\beta\Psi^0.$$ All constants depend on fixed smooth background bounds, not on the lower threshold $\beta_0$ once $\beta_0\ge1$.
  - Explanation: The scalar moving-trace adjoint separates into Euclidean leading terms and controlled angular remainders. The matrix row transpose differs from the scalar adjoint by an angular operator of lower order.
- **claim-weighted-angular-raising** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-raising)): Let $n\ge3$, and suppose <a href="#ang:profile-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:profile-bounds">[ang:profile-bounds]</a> holds on a fixed base region with uniformly bounded reference geometry. There are $\beta_0$ and $c>0$, depending only on that geometry and the constants in <a href="#ang:profile-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:profile-bounds">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>, such that every smooth, compactly base-supported function with zero fiber mean satisfies $$\label{ang:raising-estimate} \\|T\varphi\\|^2-\\|T^\flat\varphi\\|^2 \ge c\,\mathcal N(\varphi)^2.$$
  - Explanation: The difference between raising and lowering squared norms controls the strengthened weighted norm on mean-zero sphere functions. Negative depth Hessian supplies the positivity needed to absorb curvature.
- **claim-scalar-angular-coercivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-scalar-coercivity)): Fix uniform smooth bounds for the reference geometry and scalar comparison equations, and the constants in <a href="#ang:profile-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:profile-bounds">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>. Suppose $P_0$ is the scalar operator <a href="#ang:P0" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:P0">[ang:P0]</a>, with the scalar trace operators of Lemma <a href="#ang:trace" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ang:trace">17</a>. There exist $\beta_0$ sufficiently large, $\varepsilon_0>0$ and $C<\infty$, depending only on these fixed bounds, such that <a href="#ang:smallness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:smallness">[ang:smallness]</a> with $\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0$ implies $$\label{ang:coercive-estimate} \mathcal N(\varphi)\le C\\|P_0\varphi\\|$$ for every smooth, compactly base-supported function $\varphi$ with zero mean on each sphere. These constants are independent of the shrinking parameter. Only the eight indicated derivatives of the small tensor $h$ are required.
  - Explanation: The scalar comparison operator controls the strengthened norm on compactly base-supported mean-zero functions. Its real scalar principal coefficients allow the commutator cancellations needed to retain the principal perturbations.
- **claim-matrix-angular-coercivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-system-coercivity)): Fix uniform smooth bounds for the harmonic-frame systems and the reference geometry, and the constants in <a href="#ang:profile-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:profile-bounds">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>. There are $\beta_0$ sufficiently large, $\varepsilon_0>0$, and $C$, depending only on these bounds, such that <a href="#ang:smallness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ang:smallness">[ang:smallness]</a> with $\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0$ implies $$\mathcal N(\varphi)\le C\\|P\varphi\\|_{L^2}$$ for every smooth compactly base-supported matrix function $\varphi$ with zero fiber mean. The constants are independent of the shrinking parameter and of sufficiently small spatial dilations.
  - Explanation: Scalar coercivity applied entrywise extends to the matrix operator by absorbing the trace and drift differences. The constants remain uniform across shrinking radii and sufficiently small spatial dilations.
- **claim-uniform-matrix-transfer-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-ang-density)): Choose the fixed geometry as in Section <a href="#sec:spheres" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:spheres">4</a>, then choose $p_*$ sufficiently large, $\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small, and the radius amplitude $R_*>0$ sufficiently small that $r_\delta\beta_\delta^2\le\varepsilon$ for $0<\delta\le1$. For all sufficiently small spatial dilations there is a constant $C$, independent of the dilation and $\delta$, such that $$\label{ang:density-bound} \\|\chi\psi_\delta\\|_{L^2(S_\gamma Y)}\le C$$ whenever $\sum_{\|\alpha\|\le8}\|\partial^\alpha h\| \le\varepsilon r_\delta$ on $Y$. Here $\chi$ is the fixed cutoff from <a href="#sph:cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sph:cutoff">[sph:cutoff]</a> and $\psi_\delta$ is the left-acting matrix density of Proposition <a href="#sph:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sph:transfer">15</a>.
  - Explanation: Subtracting the identity mass makes the localized density mean zero, so system coercivity and the cutoff-region bounds give a uniform bound for the actual transfer density.
- **claim-uniform-selected-harmonic-jet-spanning** (construction, [source](#stmt-mat-spanning)): For all sufficiently small $\lambda$ and every $y\in Y$, the vectors $$\big(\partial_i v_{1,\nu}(y), \partial_i\partial_j v_{1,\nu}(y)\big), \qquad 1\le\nu\le N,$$ span, over $\mathbb C$, the space of pairs $(p,H)$ with $p_i\in\mathbb C^2$ and $H_{ij}=H_{ji}\in\mathbb C^2$ satisfying $$a^{ij}H_{ij}+B_1^ip_i=0. \label{mat:jet-constraint}$$ The coefficients realizing prescribed such data may be chosen by a smooth right inverse, with uniform bounds to every fixed order.
  - Explanation: A fixed finite family of rescaled harmonic columns spans precisely the allowed gradient and Hessian data at each point. A smooth uniformly bounded right inverse realizes prescribed admissible jets.
- **claim-weighted-displacement-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-mat-moments)): Under <a href="#mat:provisional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mat:provisional">[mat:provisional]</a>, $$\\|\chi D/r\\|_{L^2(Y)}\le C, \qquad \\|\chi R_\nu/r^2\\|_{L^2(Y)}\le C, \qquad 1\le\nu\le N. \label{mat:weighted-moments}$$ The constants are uniform in $\delta$ and small $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: Taylor expansion of the bounded transfer density controls the displacement after division by the radius and the selected-pair remainders after division by the squared radius.
- **claim-pointwise-moment-interpolation** (bound, [source](#stmt-mat-interpolation)): Choose $$d_*=10+\frac n2,\qquad \eta=\frac1{2d_*},\qquad p_*>2d_*,\qquad m\ge\lceil10+3d_*\rceil.$$ There is a fixed $\rho_0>0$ such that, if $y\in K$ and $r(y)<\rho_0$, then $$\sum_{\|\alpha\|\le10}\|\partial^\alpha D(y)\|\le Cr(y)^{1/2}, \qquad \sum_{\|\alpha\|\le10}\|\partial^\alpha R_\nu(y)\| \le Cr(y)^{3/2}. \label{mat:pointwise}$$ The threshold and constants are independent of $\delta$ and small $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: Local Taylor interpolation converts the weighted integral estimates into pointwise derivative estimates. Radius comparability on the interpolation ball follows from the profile Lipschitz bound.
- **claim-projected-matrix-displacement-equation** (bound, [source](#stmt-mat-projected-equation)): There is a smooth matrix-valued symmetric tensor $\mathcal E$ such that $$h^{ij}I+ \Pi\big(a^{li}\partial_lD^j+a^{lj}\partial_lD^i +\mathcal B^{ij}(D)\big) =\mathcal E^{ij}. \label{mat:projected}$$ For $y\in K$ with $r(y)<\rho_0$, $$\sum_{\|\alpha\|\le8}\|\partial^\alpha\mathcal E(y)\| \le Cr(y)^{3/2}. \label{mat:E-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Testing the comparison identity against selected trace-free Hessians eliminates the common gradient coefficient and yields the projected displacement equation with a controlled error tensor.
- **claim-conformal-killing-finite-type-prolongation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-mat-finite-type)): Let $n\ge3$, let $a=\gamma^{-1}$ be a smooth positive definite tensor on a coordinate domain, and let $V$ be a finite-dimensional real vector space. Suppose that a $V$-valued vector field $W$ satisfies $$\Pi\big(a^{li}\partial_lW^j+a^{lj}\partial_lW^i\big) +C^{ij}(W)=E^{ij},$$ where $C$ is a smooth real-linear zeroth-order operator. Every third derivative of $W$ is then a smooth linear combination of derivatives of $W$ through order two and derivatives of $E$ through order two. The coefficient bounds are uniform when $a$ is uniformly elliptic and the indicated background coefficients range in bounded smooth sets.
  - Explanation: The prolonged conformal Killing principal part is injective on third-derivative tensors. A smooth left inverse expresses every third derivative using lower jets and derivatives of the forcing.
- **claim-strict-principal-tensor-improvement** (bound, [source](#stmt-mat-improvement)): With the fixed choices in Lemma <a href="#mat:interpolation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mat:interpolation">24</a>, there are constants $C$ and $\rho_0>0$, uniform for sufficiently small $\lambda$, such that <a href="#mat:provisional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mat:provisional">[mat:provisional]</a> implies $$H_8(y)\le C r_\delta(y)^{3/2} \qquad\text{if }y\in K\text{ and }r_\delta(y)<\rho_0. \label{mat:improved-h}$$ On the same set, the derivatives of $D$ through order nine are bounded by $Cr_\delta^{3/2}$.
  - Explanation: Finite-type prolongation and integration from the known lower region improve the provisional principal-tensor estimate by a strictly higher radius power and strengthen the displacement derivative estimates.
- **claim-local-normalized-operator-equality** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-mat-local)): In the contact coordinates and harmonic frames of Proposition <a href="#bdy:contact" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bdy:contact">6</a>, there is a neighborhood of the origin on which the two metrics are smoothly conformal and the harmonic-frame equations, multiplied by the positive scalars making their principal tensors equal, coincide. All the finitely many selected pairs of harmonic-frame columns agree on that neighborhood.
  - Explanation: A fixed dilation and an open-and-closed argument preserve the provisional estimate for all positive shrinking parameters. Collapsing the radii then identifies the normalized equations and all selected harmonic pairs near the contact point.
- **claim-exact-local-unitary-identification** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-glob-conformal)): In the contact coordinates and unitary frames of Proposition <a href="#bdy:contact" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bdy:contact">6</a>, suppose that on a connected neighborhood $\Omega$ of zero the metrics satisfy $g_2=e^{2\omega}g_1$, and that the equations in the harmonic frames $F_j$, after scalar normalization to the same principal part, have equal first-order coefficients. Then $$g_2=g_1,\qquad J_{\mathrm{loc}}=F_1F_2^{-1}\in U(2),\qquad d_{A_1}(J_{\mathrm{loc}}u)=J_{\mathrm{loc}}d_{A_2}u \quad\text{on }\Omega.$$ Moreover this identification agrees with the old match on the known side and matches all corresponding source evaluations throughout $\Omega$.
  - Explanation: Equality of the normalized harmonic-frame operators makes the conformal factor satisfy a scalar harmonic equation. Unique continuation removes that factor, and parallel transport proves that the resulting connection identification is unitary.
- **claim-global-interior-isometry** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-glob-interior)): The maximal match of Lemma <a href="#bdy:matches" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bdy:matches">5</a> is defined on $N_2^\circ$, and its base map is an onto isometry $\Phi:N_2^\circ\to N_1^\circ$.
  - Explanation: Exact local continuation rules out every interior frontier of the maximal match. Compactness and source-value surjectivity make its image relatively closed, giving an onto interior isometry.
- **claim-smooth-boundary-completion-and-patch-identity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-glob-boundary)): The interior maps $(\Phi,J)$ extend smoothly to the extended manifolds, with $\Phi:N_2\to N_1$ a diffeomorphism and $J$ a unitary bundle map intertwining the connections. They preserve the original copies of $M$ and satisfy $$\Phi\|_\Gamma=\operatorname{Id},\qquad J\|_\Gamma=I.$$
  - Explanation: Metric completion and normal flows extend the interior maps smoothly to the boundary. Boundary Cauchy uniqueness and arbitrary patch-supported traces force both the base map and the unitary bundle map to fix the whole measured patch.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bdy-cap",
        "exact_quote": "There are compact connected smooth extensions $N_j$ of the original manifold, with nonempty smooth boundary, and a common connected open exterior cap $E\\subset N_j^\\circ$, attached through a nonempty open patch $P\\Subset\\Gamma$, such that the extended metrics and connections agree on $E$. For each $f\\in C_c^\\infty(E;\\mathbb C^2)$, the solutions $L_{j,D}^{-1}f$ agree on $E$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bdy-evaluations",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be a compact connected smooth manifold of dimension $n\\ge2$ with nonempty boundary, let $L=d_A^{*g}d_A$, and let $U\\Subset N^\\circ$ be a nonempty open source set.*\n\n1.  *At each interior point, $f\\mapsto L_D^{-1}f(p)$ is onto $\\mathbb C^2$.         "anchor": "stmt-bdy-contact",
        "exact_quote": "If the maximal domain $W$ has an interior frontier, there are a point $p\\in\\partial W\\cap N_2^\\circ$, a point $q\\in N_1^\\circ$, and charts about $p,q$, both with coordinate range a neighborhood of $0\\in\\mathbb R^n$, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Both charts avoid $\\overline{U_0}$. The old match is coordinate identity on its domain in these charts. That domain contains a region $x_n<\\kappa(x')$, where $\\kappa$ is smooth, $\\kappa(0)=0$, and [(bdy:limit-match)](#bdy:limit-match)$d\\kappa(0)=0$.*\n\n2.  *In smooth local unitary frames, the metrics, connection matrices, and Green matrices agree on the known region (for the Green matrices, on its square off the diagonal), and $g_1(0)=g_2(0)=I_n$.*\n\n\n3.  *There are invertible matrix functions $F_j$ with harmonic columns, chosen as corresponding source solutions. In these harmonic frames there are corresponding harmonic matrix functions $X_j^l$, $1\\le l\\le n$, satisfying $$\\label{bdy:trace-coordinates}\n     \\tfrac12\\operatorname{Re}\\operatorname{tr}X_j^l(x)=x^l,\n     \\qquad X_j^l(0)=0,\n     \\qquad \\partial_iX_j^l(0)=\\delta_i^l I.$$*\n\n4.  *There are finitely many further corresponding harmonic column functions with zero value and gradient at $0$, whose Hessians at $0$ span the space of symmetric $\\mathbb C^2$-valued tensors $H$ satisfying $\\sum_i H_{ii}=0$. All these paired functions, including the frames, agree on the known region and have identical jets at $0$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cross-series",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D_i$ be bounded connected coordinate domains, and let $S_i\\Subset D_i$ be nonempty open sets. Suppose a product solution $F$ is given consistently on $$(D_1\\times S_2)\\cup(S_1\\times D_2),$$ with the sum of its two $L^2$ norms at most $M$. Suppose $O_i\\Subset D_i$ are open sets and every $V_i$-valued solution $w$ of $\\mathsf L_iw=0$ on $D_i$ satisfies $$\\label{cross:interpolation-input}\n \\|w\\|_{L^2(O_i)}\n \\le C\\|w\\|_{L^2(S_i)}^{\\gamma_i}\n          \\|w\\|_{L^2(D_i)}^{1-\\gamma_i},\n \\qquad 0<\\gamma_i<1,\\qquad \\gamma_1+\\gamma_2>1.$$ Then $F$ extends as a product solution to $O_1\\times O_2$, agreeing with the two given pieces on their intersections. Its $C^m$ norm on every compact subset of the output product is at most $C_mM$. These constants are uniform when geometric margins, ellipticity, the required coefficient bounds, interpolation constants, and a positive lower bound for $\\gamma_1+\\gamma_2-1$ are uniform. A specified output order requires only finitely many coefficient bounds.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho$ be smooth near $z_0=(x_0,y_0)$, with $\\rho(z_0)=0$ and $d_x\\rho,d_y\\rho\\ne0$. Put $$a_i=\\frac{\\nabla_i\\rho}{|\\nabla_i\\rho|^2},\\qquad H=\\operatorname{Hess}\\rho.$$ Suppose that at $z_0$ $$\\label{cross:hessian-test}\n H((a_1,-a_2),(a_1,-a_2))\n +H((v_1,v_2),(v_1,v_2))>0\n \\quad\n \\left(v_i\\perp a_i,\\ |v_i|=|a_i|\\right).$$ Every product solution given on $\\{\\rho>0\\}$ near $z_0$ extends to a neighborhood of $z_0$, agreeing on a smaller part of that side. In fact, the construction uses only two compact product sets in $\\{\\rho>\\varepsilon\\}$ for some $\\varepsilon>0$. The neighborhoods and estimates on smaller neighborhoods are uniform under small smooth perturbations preserving the strict hypotheses, provided the data on those compact sets are bounded.",

In fact, the construction uses only two compact product sets in $\\{\\rho>\\varepsilon\\}$ for some $\\varepsilon>0$.         "anchor": "stmt-cross-propagation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a region in a product patch has smooth coordinates $(z,a)$ near $B\\times[a_-,a_+]$, where $B$ is a compact base, possibly with boundary or corners. A single product solution is given on an open set $\\mathcal S$ containing neighborhoods of the top and lateral boundary. Assume that $\\mathcal S$ is upward closed in $a$ at fixed $z$. If every level $a=\\text{constant}$ satisfies Proposition <a href=\"#cross:criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cross:criterion\">8</a> outside $\\mathcal S$, with the positive side pointing toward increasing $a$, the solution extends to a neighborhood of the cylinder, agreeing on $\\mathcal S$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cross-initial",
        "exact_quote": "In this setting, let $n\\ge3$ and fix $B>0$, $d>0$ and $\\eta>0$. For all sufficiently small $\\lambda$, the data <a href=\"#cross:mixed-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cross:mixed-data\">[cross:mixed-data]</a> have a product-solution continuation to a neighborhood of $$\\{(x,y):|x|,|y|\\le B,\\ |x-y|\\ge d\\}.$$ Every fixed $C^m$ norm on a smaller neighborhood is bounded uniformly in $\\lambda$. It agrees with $G_{1,\\lambda}$ when $y\\in W_\\lambda$ and with $G_{2,\\lambda}$ when $x\\in W_\\lambda$, on the corresponding overlaps. In particular these agreements hold on the fixed truncated lower strips $y_n<-\\eta$ and $x_n<-\\eta$, respectively. The smallness threshold for $\\lambda$ and the constants may depend on $B,d,\\eta,m$.",

      },

        "exact_quote": "For the geometry just fixed and all sufficiently small $\\lambda$, the profiles <a href=\"#sph:radius\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:radius\">[sph:radius]</a> satisfy $$\\label{sph:profile-bounds}\n \\beta\\ge cp_*,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Hess}_\\gamma f_*\\le-c\\beta\\gamma,\n \\qquad |\\partial^j f_*|\\le C_j\\beta^j\\quad(j\\ge1),$$ uniformly in $0<\\delta\\le1$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sph-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href=\"#sph:continuation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sph:continuation\">14</a>, there is a smooth matrix function $\\psi=\\psi_\\delta$ on the unit sphere bundle over $Y^\\circ$ such that $$\\label{sph:density-representation}\n T_yv=\\int_{S_y}\\psi(y,\\omega)\n       v\\bigl(\\exp_y^\\gamma(r(y)\\omega)\\bigr)\\,d\\sigma_y(\\omega).$$ For every $y_0\\in Y^\\circ$ and every first harmonic-frame function $v$ defined near $\\overline{B_\\gamma(y_0,r(y_0))}$, the function $y\\mapsto T_yv$ is second harmonic-frame harmonic on a neighborhood of $y_0$. For each pair $(v_1,v_2)$ of first and second harmonic-frame functions defined on the fixed coordinate neighborhoods and agreeing on the known side, $$\\label{sph:reproduction}\n \\int_{S_y}\\psi(y,\\omega)\\,d\\sigma_y(\\omega)=I,\n \\qquad T_yv_1=v_2(y).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-365.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

For each pair $(v_1,v_2)$ of first and second harmonic-frame functions defined on the fixed coordinate neighborhoods and agreeing on the known side, $$\\label{sph:reproduction}\n \\int_{S_y}\\psi(y,\\omega)\\,d\\sigma_y(\\omega)=I,\n \\qquad T_yv_1=v_2(y).$$",

      "exact_statement": "Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href=\"#sph:continuation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sph:continuation\">14</a>, there is a smooth matrix function $\\psi=\\psi_\\delta$ on the unit sphere bundle over $Y^\\circ$ such that $$\\label{sph:density-representation}\n T_yv=\\int_{S_y}\\psi(y,\\omega)\n       v\\bigl(\\exp_y^\\gamma(r(y)\\omega)\\bigr)\\,d\\sigma_y(\\omega).$$ For every $y_0\\in Y^\\circ$ and every first harmonic-frame function $v$ defined near $\\overline{B_\\gamma(y_0,r(y_0))}$, the function $y\\mapsto T_yv$ is second harmonic-frame harmonic on a neighborhood of $y_0$. For each pair $(v_1,v_2)$ of first and second harmonic-frame functions defined on the fixed coordinate neighborhoods and agreeing on the known side, $$\\label{sph:reproduction}\n \\int_{S_y}\\psi(y,\\omega)\\,d\\sigma_y(\\omega)=I,\n \\qquad T_yv_1=v_2(y).$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ang-trace",
        "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>–<a href=\"#ang:smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:smallness\">[ang:smallness]</a>, $$\\label{ang:C-expansion}\n \\mathcal C_{i,0}^*\n =r^{-1}\\mathsf A_i^*+f_i\\mathsf B+E_i+F_i,\n \\qquad E_i\\in r\\Psi^1,\\quad F_i\\in\\Psi^0.$$ The normalized remainders $r^{-1}E_i,F_i$ have the allowance $C_j\\beta^j$ through six base derivatives. Moreover, on rows, $$\\label{ang:trace-difference}\n K_i:=\\mathcal C_i^t-\\mathcal C_{i,0}^*\n       \\in\\Psi^0,\\qquad \\nabla K_i\\in\\beta\\Psi^0.$$ All constants depend on fixed smooth background bounds, not on the lower threshold $\\beta_0$ once $\\beta_0\\ge1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-365.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>–<a href=\"#ang:smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:smallness\">[ang:smallness]</a>, $$\\label{ang:C-expansion}\n \\mathcal C_{i,0}^*\n =r^{-1}\\mathsf A_i^*+f_i\\mathsf B+E_i+F_i,\n \\qquad E_i\\in r\\Psi^1,\\quad F_i\\in\\Psi^0.$$ The normalized remainders $r^{-1}E_i,F_i$ have the allowance $C_j\\beta^j$ through six base derivatives.           "source_id": "PM-365.1"

      "exact_statement": "Under <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>–<a href=\"#ang:smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:smallness\">[ang:smallness]</a>, $$\\label{ang:C-expansion}\n \\mathcal C_{i,0}^*\n =r^{-1}\\mathsf A_i^*+f_i\\mathsf B+E_i+F_i,\n \\qquad E_i\\in r\\Psi^1,\\quad F_i\\in\\Psi^0.$$ The normalized remainders $r^{-1}E_i,F_i$ have the allowance $C_j\\beta^j$ through six base derivatives. Moreover, on rows, $$\\label{ang:trace-difference}\n K_i:=\\mathcal C_i^t-\\mathcal C_{i,0}^*\n       \\in\\Psi^0,\\qquad \\nabla K_i\\in\\beta\\Psi^0.$$ All constants depend on fixed smooth background bounds, not on the lower threshold $\\beta_0$ once $\\beta_0\\ge1$.",
      "explanation": "The scalar moving-trace adjoint separates into Euclidean leading terms and controlled angular remainders. The matrix row transpose differs from the scalar adjoint by an angular operator of lower order.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "ang:profile-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Use the reference metric and normalized inverse matrices from <a href=\"#sph:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:normalization\">[sph:normalization]</a>. The harmonic-frame convention <a href=\"#bdy:harmonic-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bdy:harmonic-normalization\">[bdy:harmonic-normalization]</a>, with the multipliers $c_j$ in <a href=\"#sph:frame-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:frame-normalization\">[sph:frame-normalization]</a>, gives equations whose second-derivative coefficient matrices are positive definite: $$\\label{ang:frame-equations}\n \\mathcal L_1=a^{ij}\\partial_i\\partial_j+B_1^i\\partial_i,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal L_2=Q^{ij}\\partial_i\\partial_j+B_2^i\\partial_i.$$ The $B_j^i$ are complex $2\\times2$ matrices acting on columns from the left. There is no zeroth-order term because each column of the defining frame $F_j$ is harmonic. All coefficients have uniform smooth bounds on the fixed coordinate neighborhood for sufficiently small spatial dilations. Suppress the dilation and shrinking parameters for now, and write $$r=r_\\delta,\\qquad f_*=\\log r,\\qquad\n \\beta=|df_*|_\\gamma,\\qquad f_i=\\nabla_i f_*.$$ The profile bounds supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#sph:profiles\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sph:profiles\">11</a> have the form $$\\label{ang:profile-bounds}\n \\beta\\ge\\beta_0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Hess}_\\gamma f_*\\le-c_0\\beta\\gamma,\\qquad\n |\\nabla^j f_*|\\le C_j\\beta^j\\quad(1\\le j\\le8).$$ The lower threshold $\\beta_0$ can be increased by increasing $p_*$. Throughout this section we impose $$\\label{ang:smallness}\n \\sum_{|\\alpha|\\le8}|\\partial_y^\\alpha h|\\le\\varepsilon r,\n \\qquad r\\beta^2\\le\\varepsilon.$$ The first is the bootstrap assumption of Section <a href=\"#sec:spheres\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:spheres\">4</a>; the second is ensured by the choice of radius amplitude.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },

        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge3$, and suppose <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a> holds on a fixed base region with uniformly bounded reference geometry. There are $\\beta_0$ and $c>0$, depending only on that geometry and the constants in <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>, such that every smooth, compactly base-supported function with zero fiber mean satisfies $$\\label{ang:raising-estimate}\n \\|T\\varphi\\|^2-\\|T^\\flat\\varphi\\|^2\n       \\ge c\\,\\mathcal N(\\varphi)^2.$$",

      "exact_statement": "Fix uniform smooth bounds for the harmonic-frame systems and the reference geometry, and the constants in <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>. There are $\\beta_0$ sufficiently large, $\\varepsilon_0>0$, and $C$, depending only on these bounds, such that <a href=\"#ang:smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:smallness\">[ang:smallness]</a> with $\\varepsilon\\le\\varepsilon_0$ implies $$\\mathcal N(\\varphi)\\le C\\|P\\varphi\\|_{L^2}$$ for every smooth compactly base-supported matrix function $\\varphi$ with zero fiber mean. The constants are independent of the shrinking parameter and of sufficiently small spatial dilations.",
      "explanation": "Scalar coercivity applied entrywise extends to the matrix operator by absorbing the trace and drift differences. The constants remain uniform across shrinking radii and sufficiently small spatial dilations.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-system-coercivity",
          "exact_quote": "Fix uniform smooth bounds for the harmonic-frame systems and the reference geometry, and the constants in",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "ang:smallness",
          "exact_quote": "Use the reference metric and normalized inverse matrices from <a href=\"#sph:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:normalization\">[sph:normalization]</a>. The harmonic-frame convention <a href=\"#bdy:harmonic-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bdy:harmonic-normalization\">[bdy:harmonic-normalization]</a>, with the multipliers $c_j$ in <a href=\"#sph:frame-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:frame-normalization\">[sph:frame-normalization]</a>, gives equations whose second-derivative coefficient matrices are positive definite: $$\\label{ang:frame-equations}\n \\mathcal L_1=a^{ij}\\partial_i\\partial_j+B_1^i\\partial_i,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal L_2=Q^{ij}\\partial_i\\partial_j+B_2^i\\partial_i.$$ The $B_j^i$ are complex $2\\times2$ matrices acting on columns from the left. There is no zeroth-order term because each column of the defining frame $F_j$ is harmonic. All coefficients have uniform smooth bounds on the fixed coordinate neighborhood for sufficiently small spatial dilations. Suppress the dilation and shrinking parameters for now, and write $$r=r_\\delta,\\qquad f_*=\\log r,\\qquad\n \\beta=|df_*|_\\gamma,\\qquad f_i=\\nabla_i f_*.$$ The profile bounds supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#sph:profiles\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sph:profiles\">11</a> have the form $$\\label{ang:profile-bounds}\n \\beta\\ge\\beta_0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Hess}_\\gamma f_*\\le-c_0\\beta\\gamma,\\qquad\n |\\nabla^j f_*|\\le C_j\\beta^j\\quad(1\\le j\\le8).$$ The lower threshold $\\beta_0$ can be increased by increasing $p_*$. Throughout this section we impose $$\\label{ang:smallness}\n \\sum_{|\\alpha|\\le8}|\\partial_y^\\alpha h|\\le\\varepsilon r,\n \\qquad r\\beta^2\\le\\varepsilon.$$ The first is the bootstrap assumption of Section <a href=\"#sec:spheres\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:spheres\">4</a>; the second is ensured by the choice of radius amplitude.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-system-coercivity",
          "exact_quote": "with $\\varepsilon\\le\\varepsilon_0$",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ang-density",
        "exact_quote": "Choose the fixed geometry as in Section <a href=\"#sec:spheres\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:spheres\">4</a>, then choose $p_*$ sufficiently large, $\\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small, and the radius amplitude $R_*>0$ sufficiently small that $r_\\delta\\beta_\\delta^2\\le\\varepsilon$ for $0<\\delta\\le1$. For all sufficiently small spatial dilations there is a constant $C$, independent of the dilation and $\\delta$, such that $$\\label{ang:density-bound}\n \\|\\chi\\psi_\\delta\\|_{L^2(S_\\gamma Y)}\\le C$$ whenever $\\sum_{|\\alpha|\\le8}|\\partial^\\alpha h|\n\\le\\varepsilon r_\\delta$ on $Y$.         "source_id": "PM-365.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mat-spanning",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small $\\lambda$ and every $y\\in Y$, the vectors $$\\big(\\partial_i v_{1,\\nu}(y),\n             \\partial_i\\partial_j v_{1,\\nu}(y)\\big),\n \\qquad 1\\le\\nu\\le N,$$ span, over $\\mathbb C$, the space of pairs $(p,H)$ with $p_i\\in\\mathbb C^2$ and $H_{ij}=H_{ji}\\in\\mathbb C^2$ satisfying $$a^{ij}H_{ij}+B_1^ip_i=0.\n \\label{mat:jet-constraint}$$ The coefficients realizing prescribed such data may be chosen by a smooth right inverse, with uniform bounds to every fixed order.",
        "source_id": "PM-365.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-365.1"

      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "mat:provisional",
          "exact_quote": "Throughout the next three subsections we fix $0<\\delta\\le1$ and assume the provisional comparison $$H_8(y):=\\sum_{|\\alpha|\\le8}|\\partial^\\alpha h(y)|\n                    \\le\\varepsilon r_\\delta(y),\\qquad y\\in Y.\n \\label{mat:provisional}$$ Write $r=r_\\delta$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mat-finite-type",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge3$, let $a=\\gamma^{-1}$ be a smooth positive definite tensor on a coordinate domain, and let $V$ be a finite-dimensional real vector space. Suppose that a $V$-valued vector field $W$ satisfies $$\\Pi\\big(a^{li}\\partial_lW^j+a^{lj}\\partial_lW^i\\big)\n                          +C^{ij}(W)=E^{ij},$$ where $C$ is a smooth real-linear zeroth-order operator. Every third derivative of $W$ is then a smooth linear combination of derivatives of $W$ through order two and derivatives of $E$ through order two. The coefficient bounds are uniform when $a$ is uniformly elliptic and the indicated background coefficients range in bounded smooth sets.",
        "source_id": "PM-365.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mat-finite-type",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge3$, let $a=\\gamma^{-1}$ be a smooth positive definite tensor on a coordinate domain, and let $V$ be a finite-dimensional real vector space. Every third derivative of $W$ is then a smooth linear combination of derivatives of $W$ through order two and derivatives of $E$ through order two. The coefficient bounds are uniform when $a$ is uniformly elliptic and the indicated background coefficients range in bounded smooth sets.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Geometric domain and measured patch** ([source](#eq:DN)): Let $M$ be a compact connected smooth manifold of dimension $n\geq3$, with nonempty smooth boundary, and let $\Gamma\subset\partial M$ be a nonempty relatively open proper subset.
- **Trivial Hermitian bundle** ([source](#eq:DN)): The bundle is the trivial Hermitian bundle $M\times\mathbb C^2$.
- **Unitary connection** ([source](#eq:DN)): A smooth unitary connection is written $d_A=d+A$, where $$A\in C^\infty(M;T^*M\otimes\mathfrak u(2)),\qquad \mathfrak u(2)=\{B\in\mathbb C^{2\times2}:B^*=-B\}.$$
- **Connection Laplacian** ([source](#eq:DN)): For a smooth Riemannian metric $g$, write $L_{g,A}=d_A^{*g}d_A$.
- **Localized boundary energy form** ([source](#eq:DN)): The measured object is the sesquilinear energy form $$\label{eq:DN} \langle\Lambda_{g,A,\Gamma}f,h\rangle =\int_M\langle d_Au_f^{g,A},d_Au_h^{g,A}\rangle_g\,dV_g, \qquad f,h\in C_c^\infty(\Gamma;\mathbb C^2).$$
- **Gauge action** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A unitary gauge $V:M\to U(2)$ changes the connection to $$\label{bdy:gauge} A^V=V^{-1}AV+V^{-1}dV,\qquad d_{A^V}(V^{-1}u)=V^{-1}d_Au.$$
- **Source evaluation maps** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For $f\in C_c^\infty(U_0;\mathbb C^2)$, set $$\label{bdy:source-evaluations} w_{j,f}=L_{j,D}^{-1}f,\qquad I_j(p)f=w_{j,f}(p).$$
- **Match** ([source](#bdy:matching)): A *match* consists of a local isometry $\Phi:W\to N_1^\circ$, with $W\subset N_2^\circ$ connected, open, and containing $E$, and a smooth unitary bundle map $J$ from the second fiber at $p$ to the first fiber at $\Phi(p)$.
- **Normalized harmonic-frame equation** ([source](#bdy:harmonic-normalization)): For the local argument, our convention for a normalized equation in a harmonic frame is $$\label{bdy:harmonic-normalization} \mathcal L_jv=-c_jF_j^{-1}L_{g_j,A_j}(F_jv),\qquad c_j>0.$$
- **Product solution** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A *product solution* is a $V_1\otimes V_2$-valued function $F(x,y)$ satisfying $$(\mathsf L_1^x\otimes I)F=0, \qquad (I\otimes\mathsf L_2^y)F=0.$$
- **Strict Carleman weight** ([source](#cross:strict-weight)): For a metric $g$, call a smooth real function $\theta$ a *strict weight* at a point where $d\theta\ne0$ if $$\label{cross:strict-weight} \operatorname{Hess}_g\theta(e,e) +\operatorname{Hess}_g\theta(v,v)>0 \quad\text{for all }v\perp e,\ \|v\|_g=1, \qquad e=\frac{\nabla^g\theta}{\|\nabla^g\theta\|_g}.$$
- **Reference metric and trace-normalized inverse tensors** ([source](#sph:normalization)): For a sufficiently large fixed $L$, define $$\label{sph:normalization} \gamma=e^{-2Lt_0}g_1,\qquad a=\gamma^{-1},\qquad Q=\frac{n g_2^{-1}}{\operatorname{tr}(\gamma g_2^{-1})}, \qquad h=Q-a.$$
- **Trace-free principal-tensor difference** ([source](#sph:trace-free)): $$\label{sph:trace-free} \operatorname{tr}(\gamma h)=0,\qquad h\longrightarrow0 \quad\hbox{in every fixed smooth norm as }\lambda\downarrow0.$$
- **Concave depth** ([source](#sph:depth)): Define a depth function by $$\label{sph:depth} t(y)=\int_{1/8}^{t_0(y)}e^{-2Ls}\,ds.$$
- **Regularized radius profiles** ([source](#sph:radius)): For an integer $p_*\ge2$, an amplitude $R_*>0$, and $0<\delta\le1$, put $$\label{sph:radius} \ell_\delta(t)=\delta\log(1+e^{t/\delta}),\qquad r_\delta(y)=R_*\ell_\delta(t(y))^{p_*},\qquad f_*=\log r_\delta,\qquad \beta=\|df_*\|_\gamma.$$ We use the $\gamma$-geodesic ball with center $y$ and radius $r_\delta(y)$. These balls shrink to a point on $\{t\le0\}$, including a neighborhood of the contact point. At positive depths bounded away from zero, their radii retain a positive lower bound. This will let us place cutoff derivatives where the transfer is already controlled.
- **Harmonic transfer functional** ([source](#sph:transfer-definition)): For a first harmonic-frame function $v$, define $$\label{sph:transfer-definition} T_yv=F_2(y)^{-1}S(y;F_1v).$$
- **Bilinear row transpose** ([source](#ang:row-transpose)): If $\mathcal C$ is a sphere operator on columns, its *bilinear row transpose* $\mathcal C^t$ is defined by $$\label{ang:row-transpose} \int_{S_y}(\mathcal C^t\psi)V\,d\sigma_y =\int_{S_y}\psi(\mathcal C V)\,d\sigma_y.$$
- **Radius-independent matrix displacement** ([source](#mat:displacement)): Define matrices $D^i(y)$, $1\le i\le n$, by the linear system $$\widehat X_2^l-\widehat X_1^l =\sum_iD^i\partial_i\widehat X_1^l, \qquad 1\le l\le n. \label{mat:displacement}$$ The map on the right sends the $n$ matrices $D^i$ to $n$ matrices by right multiplication with the indicated coefficients. It converges uniformly, with derivatives, to the identity map because $\partial_i\widehat X_1^l\to\delta_i^l I$. Hence <a href="#mat:displacement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mat:displacement">[mat:displacement]</a> defines smooth $D^i$ with uniform smooth bounds. In particular, $D$ is independent of $\delta$. For each selected pair define $$R_\nu=v_{2,\nu}-v_{1,\nu}-D^i\partial_i v_{1,\nu}. \label{mat:remainder}$$ These remainders also have uniform smooth bounds and are independent of $\delta$.
- **Selected-pair remainder** ([source](#pm-h-34)): For each selected pair define $$R_\nu=v_{2,\nu}-v_{1,\nu}-D^i\partial_i v_{1,\nu}. \label{mat:remainder}$$
- **Complement to real scalar matrices** ([source](#mat:scalar-direction)): Define the real-linear projection on matrices $$q(Z)=Z-\tfrac12\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{tr}(Z)I, \qquad \mathcal V_0=\{Z\in\mathbb C^{2\times2}: \operatorname{Re}\operatorname{tr}Z=0\}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| quantity-main-dimension-threshold | Manifold dimension | ≥ | $3$ | Main inverse boundary problem |
| quantity-bundle-rank | Complex fiber rank | = | $2$ | Bundle fixed throughout the paper |
| quantity-cross-interpolation-threshold | sum of the decay exponents | > | $1$ | Interpolation exponents for the chosen observed and output sets |
| quantity-radius-weight-smallness | product of the radius and squared scaling parameter | ≤ | $\varepsilon$ | Radius amplitude chosen after the profile exponent and smallness parameter |
| quantity-provisional-principal-tensor-bound | higher-order Sobolev norm of the perturbation | ≤ | $\varepsilon r_\delta(y)$ | Fixed positive shrinking parameter |
| quantity-transfer-density-uniform-bound | localized square-integrable norm of the spinor field | ≤ | $C$ | Uniform over admissible shrinking parameters and sufficiently small spatial dilations |
| quantity-weighted-displacement-bound | localized radial square-integrable norm of D | ≤ | $C$ | Uniform in the shrinking parameter and small dilation |
| quantity-weighted-selected-remainder-bound | weighted localized square-integrable norm of the curvature term | ≤ | $C$ | Uniform in the shrinking parameter and small dilation |
| quantity-interpolation-dimension-offset | critical distance parameter | = | $10+\frac n2$ | Fixed dimension-dependent interpolation choice |
| quantity-interpolation-ball-exponent | $\eta$ | = | $\frac1{2d_*}$ | Fixed exponent for the radius of the interpolation ball |
| quantity-interpolation-profile-threshold | $p_*$ | > | $2d_*$ | Fixed profile exponent |
| quantity-interpolation-smoothness-threshold | $m$ | ≥ | $\lceil10+3d_*\rceil$ | Taylor interpolation regularity order |
| quantity-initial-pointwise-displacement-bound | sum of derivatives of D up to a fixed order | ≤ | $Cr(y)^{1/2}$ | Uniform in the shrinking parameter and sufficiently small dilation |
| quantity-pointwise-selected-remainder-bound | sum of derivatives of the curvature term up to a fixed order | ≤ | $Cr(y)^{3/2}$ | Uniform in the shrinking parameter and sufficiently small dilation |
| quantity-projected-equation-error-bound | sum of derivatives of the error term up to a fixed order | ≤ | $Cr(y)^{3/2}$ | Small-radius portion of the core |
| quantity-improved-principal-tensor-bound | higher-order Sobolev norm of the perturbation | ≤ | $C r_\delta(y)^{3/2}$ | Fixed interpolation choices and sufficiently small spatial dilation |

          "anchor": "mat:provisional",
          "exact_quote": "Throughout the next three subsections we fix $0<\\delta\\le1$ and assume the provisional comparison $$H_8(y):=\\sum_{|\\alpha|\\le8}|\\partial^\\alpha h(y)|\n                    \\le\\varepsilon r_\\delta(y),\\qquad y\\in Y.\n \\label{mat:provisional}$$ Write $r=r_\\delta$.           "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-boundary-jets** [claim](#stmt-bdy-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bdy-jets): Boundary pseudodifferential factorization and even–odd symbol separation -> Equal full metric and connection jets on the measured patch
- **step-common-cap** [claim](#stmt-bdy-cap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bdy-cap): Smooth exterior pasting and comparison of strictly minimizing Dirichlet energies -> Common exterior source solutions and Green matrices
- **step-source-jet-surjectivity** [claim](#stmt-bdy-evaluations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bdy-evaluations): Distributional transposition, unique continuation, and finite-dimensional duality -> Surjective values, joint point separation, and exact harmonic jet realization
- **step-maximal-match** [claim](#stmt-bdy-matches) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bdy-matches): Source-value uniqueness and gluing of local isometries and unitary fiber maps -> Unique injective maximal match with the matched Green identity
- **step-contact-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-bdy-contact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bdy-contact): Compactness of partner points, harmonic source coordinates, and polar frame orthonormalization -> Common contact coordinates, unitary and harmonic frames, and selected harmonic jets
- **step-cross-series** [claim](#stmt-cross-series) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-series): Compact restriction singular system, rapid singular-value decay, and interpolation -> A smooth product solution on the output product with uniform compact-subset bounds
- **step-product-hessian-criterion** [claim](#stmt-cross-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-criterion): Convex separation of Hessian forms and strict Carleman interpolation in each factor -> Local continuation across the hypersurface with perturbation-uniform margins
- **step-compact-cylinder-gluing** [claim](#stmt-cross-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-propagation): Last-level contradiction and vertical overlap identification by unique continuation -> Single-valued continuation through the cylinder
- **step-fixed-separation-green-initialization** [claim](#stmt-cross-initial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cross-initial): Crossing the limiting plane, identifying retained overlaps, and sweeping variable-radius tubes -> Uniformly bounded continued mixed Green matrix on fixed separated pairs
- **step-radius-profile-estimates** [claim](#stmt-sph-profiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-profiles): Logarithmic profile differentiation and strict reference-depth concavity -> Uniform profile bounds, monotonic radii, and amplitude smallness
- **step-strict-sphere-test** [claim](#stmt-sph-strict) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-strict): Endpoint Jacobi estimates and cancellation of leading radius-gradient Hessian terms -> Strict product extension criterion on every admissible moving sphere
- **step-sphere-family-continuation** [claim](#stmt-sph-continuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-continuation): Monotone radius deformation and compact-cylinder propagation -> Smooth mixed Green continuation near each admissible positive-radius sphere family
- **step-matrix-transfer-density** [claim](#stmt-sph-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-transfer): Transpose the ball Dirichlet boundary operator in the Green flux and use fixed surrounding surfaces -> Smooth matrix density, local output harmonicity, and reproduction with identity mass
- **step-cutoff-density-bounds** [claim](#stmt-sph-cutoff-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sph-cutoff-bounds): Smooth unit-ball Dirichlet parameter dependence and positive-depth fixed-separation estimates -> Uniform angular and first-base-derivative bounds where the cutoff varies
- **step-moving-trace-symbols** [claim](#stmt-ang-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-trace): Smooth small-ball Dirichlet factorization and parameter Taylor expansion in angular symbol topology -> Scalar trace expansion and controlled matrix trace difference
- **step-weighted-raising-estimate** [claim](#stmt-ang-raising) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-raising): Degreewise Fischer identities, covariant integration by parts, and a weighted convex combination -> Strengthened weighted norm controlled by the raising-minus-lowering energy
- **step-scalar-coercivity** [claim](#stmt-ang-scalar-coercivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-scalar-coercivity): Squared-norm comparison, scalar commutator cancellation, and weighted error-form absorption -> Uniform scalar coercivity on mean-zero sphere functions
- **step-system-coercivity** [claim](#stmt-ang-system-coercivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-system-coercivity): Ordered matrix comparison and entrywise scalar coercivity with threshold absorption -> Uniform matrix coercivity without a small drift-difference assumption
- **step-uniform-transfer-density** [claim](#stmt-ang-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ang-density): Subtract identity mass, localize the density equation, and apply system coercivity -> Uniform localized transfer-density bound as radii shrink
- **step-selected-jet-spanning** [claim](#stmt-mat-spanning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-spanning): Affine and trace-free quadratic dilation limits with a smooth full-rank right inverse -> Uniform realization of every admissible gradient and Hessian pair
- **step-weighted-moments** [claim](#stmt-mat-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-moments): Taylor expansion of the transfer and inversion of the coordinate displacement system -> Weighted integral bounds for displacement and remainders
- **step-pointwise-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-mat-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-interpolation): Radius-comparable local balls and finite-dimensional Taylor polynomial norm equivalence -> Pointwise derivative estimates for displacement and selected remainders
- **step-projected-displacement-equation** [claim](#stmt-mat-projected-equation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-projected-equation): Ordered harmonic-equation comparison and testing against realized trace-free Hessians -> Projected displacement equation with a higher-power radius error
- **step-finite-type-prolongation** [claim](#stmt-mat-finite-type) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-finite-type): Homogeneous cubic kernel exclusion and smooth left inversion of the prolonged symbol -> Third derivatives expressed through lower jets and forcing derivatives
- **step-strict-tensor-improvement** [claim](#stmt-mat-improvement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-improvement): Project away real scalar matrices, prolong, and integrate the lower-jet system along vertical segments -> Strict improvement of the principal-tensor and displacement bounds
- **step-local-equation-equality** [claim](#stmt-mat-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mat-local): Fix the dilation after all thresholds, propagate an open-and-closed bootstrap, and collapse radii -> Local conformal metric equality and equal normalized harmonic-frame equations
- **step-exact-local-identification** [claim](#stmt-glob-conformal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-conformal): Conformal first-order comparison, scalar unique continuation, and unitary parallel transport -> Exact local isometry, unitary connection identification, and all source evaluation identities
- **step-global-interior-identification** [claim](#stmt-glob-interior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-interior): Contradict maximality at any frontier and prove image closedness with source evaluations -> Bijective interior isometry with unitary connection identification
- **step-boundary-completion** [claim](#stmt-glob-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-boundary): Metric completion, boundary-normal flow, connection transport, and boundary Cauchy uniqueness -> Smooth global maps preserving the original manifold and fixing the entire measured patch
- **step-return-to-original-gauges** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-boundary-2): Compose the global bundle identification with the original boundary-identity normal gauges -> The main diffeomorphism and unitary gauge identities in the original trivializations

      "depends_on": [
        "step-cross-series"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-cross-criterion",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-cross-criterion</summary>\n\n*Proof.* We first split the product Hessian condition into a strict weight in each factor.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cross-criterion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho$ be smooth near $z_0=(x_0,y_0)$, with $\\rho(z_0)=0$ and $d_x\\rho,d_y\\rho\\ne0$. Put $$a_i=\\frac{\\nabla_i\\rho}{|\\nabla_i\\rho|^2},\\qquad H=\\operatorname{Hess}\\rho.$$ Suppose that at $z_0$ $$\\label{cross:hessian-test}\n H((a_1,-a_2),(a_1,-a_2))\n +H((v_1,v_2),(v_1,v_2))>0\n \\quad\n \\left(v_i\\perp a_i,\\ |v_i|=|a_i|\\right).$$ Every product solution given on $\\{\\rho>0\\}$ near $z_0$ extends to a neighborhood of $z_0$, agreeing on a smaller part of that side. In fact, the construction uses only two compact product sets in $\\{\\rho>\\varepsilon\\}$ for some $\\varepsilon>0$. The neighborhoods and estimates on smaller neighborhoods are uniform under small smooth perturbations preserving the strict hypotheses, provided the data on those compact sets are bounded.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-product-hessian-criterion",
      "inputs": [
        "Nonvanishing partial gradients",
        "Strict product Hessian inequality",
        "Product data on the positive side"
      ],
      "method": "Convex separation of Hessian forms and strict Carleman interpolation in each factor",
      "output": "Local continuation across the hypersurface with perturbation-uniform margins",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cross-criterion"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cross-propagation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-product-hessian-criterion"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-cross-propagation",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-cross-propagation</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Starting from the top, let $a_*$ be the infimum of levels down to which an extension agreeing with the seed has been obtained.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cross-propagation",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a region in a product patch has smooth coordinates $(z,a)$ near $B\\times[a_-,a_+]$, where $B$ is a compact base, possibly with boundary or corners. A single product solution is given on an open set $\\mathcal S$ containing neighborhoods of the top and lateral boundary. Assume that $\\mathcal S$ is upward closed in $a$ at fixed $z$. If every level $a=\\text{constant}$ satisfies Proposition <a href=\"#cross:criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cross:criterion\">8</a> outside $\\mathcal S$, with the positive side pointing toward increasing $a$, the solution extends to a neighborhood of the cylinder, agreeing on $\\mathcal S$. For fixed coordinates, compact sets and strict reference inequalities, the extension has uniform compact-subset bounds under small smooth perturbations, in terms of bounds on finitely many compact subsets of the initially given region.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-compact-cylinder-gluing",
      "inputs": [
        "Compact cylinder coordinates",
        "Upward-closed top and lateral seed",
        "Strict local extension criterion"
      ],
      "method": "Last-level contradiction and vertical overlap identification by unique continuation",
      "output": "Single-valued continuation through the cylinder",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cross-propagation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cross-initial",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-contact-coordinates",
        "step-cross-series",
        "step-product-hessian-criterion",
        "step-compact-cylinder-gluing"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-cross-initial",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-cross-initial</summary>\n\n*Proof.* For the final comparison with the full original cross, fix at the outset $$\\widehat B=B+\\eta+4,\\qquad\n \\widehat d=\\min(d/2,1/4).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cross-initial",
          "exact_quote": "In this setting, let $n\\ge3$ and fix $B>0$, $d>0$ and $\\eta>0$. For all sufficiently small $\\lambda$, the data <a href=\"#cross:mixed-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cross:mixed-data\">[cross:mixed-data]</a> have a product-solution continuation to a neighborhood of $$\\{(x,y):|x|,|y|\\le B,\\ |x-y|\\ge d\\}.$$ Every fixed $C^m$ norm on a smaller neighborhood is bounded uniformly in $\\lambda$. It agrees with $G_{1,\\lambda}$ when $y\\in W_\\lambda$ and with $G_{2,\\lambda}$ when $x\\in W_\\lambda$, on the corresponding overlaps. In particular these agreements hold on the fixed truncated lower strips $y_n<-\\eta$ and $x_n<-\\eta$, respectively. The smallness threshold for $\\lambda$ and the constants may depend on $B,d,\\eta,m$.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-fixed-separation-green-initialization",
      "inputs": [
        "Dilated contact Green data",
        "Bounded locations and fixed positive separation",
        "Small smooth perturbation of Euclidean principal metrics"
      ],
      "method": "Crossing the limiting plane, identifying retained overlaps, and sweeping variable-radius tubes",
      "output": "Uniformly bounded continued mixed Green matrix on fixed separated pairs",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cross-initial"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-sph-profiles",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-contact-coordinates"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-sph-profiles",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-sph-profiles</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Write $a_\\delta=(\\log\\ell_\\delta)'$.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sph-profiles",
          "exact_quote": "For the geometry just fixed and all sufficiently small $\\lambda$, the profiles <a href=\"#sph:radius\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:radius\">[sph:radius]</a> satisfy $$\\label{sph:profile-bounds}\n \\beta\\ge cp_*,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Hess}_\\gamma f_*\\le-c\\beta\\gamma,\n \\qquad |\\partial^j f_*|\\le C_j\\beta^j\\quad(j\\ge1),$$ uniformly in $0<\\delta\\le1$. The constants $c,C_j$ are independent of $p_*\\ge2$. For fixed $p_*,R_*$, the functions $r_\\delta^{1/p_*}$ have a uniform Lipschitz bound. The radii increase strictly with $\\delta$, converge to zero where $t\\le0$, and obey $$\\label{sph:amplitude-smallness}\n \\sup_{0<\\delta\\le1,\\ y\\in Y}r_\\delta\\beta^2\n \\longrightarrow0\\quad\\hbox{as }R_*\\downarrow0$$ for each fixed $p_*$.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-radius-profile-estimates",
      "inputs": [
        "Fixed conformal reference geometry",
        "Regularized depth profiles"
      ],
      "method": "Logarithmic profile differentiation and strict reference-depth concavity",
      "output": "Uniform profile bounds, monotonic radii, and amplitude smallness",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-sph-profiles"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-sph-strict",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-radius-profile-estimates",
        "step-product-hessian-criterion"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-sph-strict",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-sph-strict</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Put $b=\\nabla^\\gamma r$, so that $|b|=r\\beta$.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sph-strict",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r=r_\\delta$ satisfy <a href=\"#sph:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:profile-bounds\">[sph:profile-bounds]</a>, <a href=\"#sph:smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:smallness\">[sph:smallness]</a>, and <a href=\"#sph:radius-smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:radius-smallness\">[sph:radius-smallness]</a>. For $p_*$ sufficiently large and $\\varepsilon$ sufficiently small, the hypersurface $$\\rho(x,y)=\\tfrac12\\bigl(d_\\gamma(x,y)^2-r(y)^2\\bigr)=0$$ satisfies the strict criterion of Proposition <a href=\"#cross:criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cross:criterion\">8</a> for principal metrics $\\gamma$ in the first factor and $Q^{-1}$ in the second. Both partial gradients are nonzero, and the positive side is the exterior of the moving ball.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-strict-sphere-test",
      "inputs": [
        "Uniform logarithmic profile concavity",
        "Provisional principal-tensor comparison",
        "Radius-weight smallness"
      ],
      "method": "Endpoint Jacobi estimates and cancellation of leading radius-gradient Hessian terms",
      "output": "Strict product extension criterion on every admissible moving sphere",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-sph-strict"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-sph-continuation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-fixed-separation-green-initialization",
        "step-strict-sphere-test",
        "step-compact-cylinder-gluing"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-sph-continuation",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-sph-continuation</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Deform $\\eta$ from $1$ down to $\\delta$, using $(y,\\omega)$ in the unit sphere bundle of $\\gamma$ as base coordinates and $$x=\\exp_y^\\gamma(r_\\eta(y)\\omega).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sph-continuation",
          "exact_quote": "Make the choices of Remark <a href=\"#sph:choices\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sph:choices\">12</a>. For all sufficiently small $\\lambda$ and every $0<\\delta\\le1$ satisfying <a href=\"#sph:smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:smallness\">[sph:smallness]</a>, the mixed Green matrix extends to a neighborhood of $$\\{(x,y):y\\in Y,\\ d_\\gamma(x,y)=r_\\delta(y)\\}.$$ It solves the first system in $x$ and the conjugate second system in the second tensor index. On the lower center region $y_n<-1/4$ it agrees with $G_1$, and on the prescribed positive-depth region $t>t_b$ it agrees with the fixed-separation continuation of Proposition <a href=\"#cross:initial\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cross:initial\">10</a>. The extension is smooth for each positive $\\delta$.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-sphere-family-continuation",
      "inputs": [
        "Fixed-separation initialization",
        "Strict sphere geometry",
        "Lower-center and positive-depth seed regions"
      ],
      "method": "Monotone radius deformation and compact-cylinder propagation",
      "output": "Smooth mixed Green continuation near each admissible positive-radius sphere family",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-sph-continuation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-sph-transfer",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-sphere-family-continuation"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-sph-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-sph-transfer</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The Dirichlet problem on each ball is invertible.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sph-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href=\"#sph:continuation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sph:continuation\">14</a>, there is a smooth matrix function $\\psi=\\psi_\\delta$ on the unit sphere bundle over $Y^\\circ$ such that $$\\label{sph:density-representation}\n T_yv=\\int_{S_y}\\psi(y,\\omega)\n       v\\bigl(\\exp_y^\\gamma(r(y)\\omega)\\bigr)\\,d\\sigma_y(\\omega).$$ For every $y_0\\in Y^\\circ$ and every first harmonic-frame function $v$ defined near $\\overline{B_\\gamma(y_0,r(y_0))}$, the function $y\\mapsto T_yv$ is second harmonic-frame harmonic on a neighborhood of $y_0$. For each pair $(v_1,v_2)$ of first and second harmonic-frame functions defined on the fixed coordinate neighborhoods and agreeing on the known side, $$\\label{sph:reproduction}\n \\int_{S_y}\\psi(y,\\omega)\\,d\\sigma_y(\\omega)=I,\n \\qquad T_yv_1=v_2(y).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-matrix-transfer-density",
      "inputs": [
        "Continued Green matrix",
        "Invertible ball Dirichlet systems",
        "Harmonic frames"
      ],
      "method": "Transpose the ball Dirichlet boundary operator in the Green flux and use fixed surrounding surfaces",
      "output": "Smooth matrix density, local output harmonicity, and reproduction with identity mass",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-sph-transfer"
    },
    {

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ang-trace",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-radius-profile-estimates"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-ang-trace",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-ang-trace</summary>\n\n*Proof.* First regard the radius $s$ as an independent parameter.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>–<a href=\"#ang:smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:smallness\">[ang:smallness]</a>, $$\\label{ang:C-expansion}\n \\mathcal C_{i,0}^*\n =r^{-1}\\mathsf A_i^*+f_i\\mathsf B+E_i+F_i,\n \\qquad E_i\\in r\\Psi^1,\\quad F_i\\in\\Psi^0.$$ The normalized remainders $r^{-1}E_i,F_i$ have the allowance $C_j\\beta^j$ through six base derivatives. Moreover, on rows, $$\\label{ang:trace-difference}\n K_i:=\\mathcal C_i^t-\\mathcal C_{i,0}^*\n       \\in\\Psi^0,\\qquad \\nabla K_i\\in\\beta\\Psi^0.$$ All constants depend on fixed smooth background bounds, not on the lower threshold $\\beta_0$ once $\\beta_0\\ge1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-moving-trace-symbols",
      "inputs": [
