        "Profile bounds",

      ],
      "method": "Smooth small-ball Dirichlet factorization and parameter Taylor expansion in angular symbol topology",
      "output": "Scalar trace expansion and controlled matrix trace difference",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ang-trace"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ang-raising",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-radius-profile-estimates"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-ang-raising",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-ang-raising</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Both operators change angular degree by exactly one, so it suffices to prove the estimate at one input degree $k\\ge1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-raising",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge3$, and suppose <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a> holds on a fixed base region with uniformly bounded reference geometry. There are $\\beta_0$ and $c>0$, depending only on that geometry and the constants in <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>, such that every smooth, compactly base-supported function with zero fiber mean satisfies $$\\label{ang:raising-estimate}\n \\|T\\varphi\\|^2-\\|T^\\flat\\varphi\\|^2\n       \\ge c\\,\\mathcal N(\\varphi)^2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-weighted-raising-estimate",
      "inputs": [
        "Mean-zero spherical harmonic decomposition",
        "Negative logarithmic-radius Hessian",
        "Uniformly bounded reference curvature"
      ],
      "method": "Degreewise Fischer identities, covariant integration by parts, and a weighted convex combination",
      "output": "Strengthened weighted norm controlled by the raising-minus-lowering energy",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ang-raising"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ang-scalar-coercivity",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-moving-trace-symbols",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-ang-scalar-coercivity",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-ang-scalar-coercivity</summary>\n\n\n\n\n*Proof.* Let a dagger denote the full adjoint for $dV_\\gamma d\\sigma$.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-scalar-coercivity",
          "exact_quote": "Fix uniform smooth bounds for the reference geometry and scalar comparison equations, and the constants in <a href=\"#ang:profile-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:profile-bounds\">[ang:profile-bounds]</a>. Suppose $P_0$ is the scalar operator <a href=\"#ang:P0\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:P0\">[ang:P0]</a>, with the scalar trace operators of Lemma <a href=\"#ang:trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ang:trace\">17</a>. There exist $\\beta_0$ sufficiently large, $\\varepsilon_0>0$ and $C<\\infty$, depending only on these fixed bounds, such that <a href=\"#ang:smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:smallness\">[ang:smallness]</a> with $\\varepsilon\\le\\varepsilon_0$ implies $$\\label{ang:coercive-estimate}\n \\mathcal N(\\varphi)\\le C\\|P_0\\varphi\\|$$ for every smooth, compactly base-supported function $\\varphi$ with zero mean on each sphere. These constants are independent of the shrinking parameter. Only the eight indicated derivatives of the small tensor $h$ are required.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-scalar-coercivity",
      "inputs": [

      "output": "Uniform scalar coercivity on mean-zero sphere functions",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ang-scalar-coercivity"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ang-system-coercivity",
      "depends_on": [

        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-ang-system-coercivity",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-ang-system-coercivity</summary>\n\n\n*Proof.* Put $q^i=b_2^i-b_{2,0}^iI$ and use $D_i=D_{i,0}+K_i$ from <a href=\"#ang:trace-difference\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:trace-difference\">[ang:trace-difference]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"

        {

There are $\\beta_0$ sufficiently large, $\\varepsilon_0>0$, and $C$, depending only on these bounds, such that <a href=\"#ang:smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ang:smallness\">[ang:smallness]</a> with $\\varepsilon\\le\\varepsilon_0$ implies $$\\mathcal N(\\varphi)\\le C\\|P\\varphi\\|_{L^2}$$ for every smooth compactly base-supported matrix function $\\varphi$ with zero fiber mean. The constants are independent of the shrinking parameter and of sufficiently small spatial dilations.",

        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-ang-density",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-ang-density</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The reproduction identity $\\int\\psi_\\delta\\,d\\sigma_y=I$ makes $\\varphi=\\chi(\\psi_\\delta-I)$ mean zero on each sphere.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ang-density",
          "exact_quote": "Choose the fixed geometry as in Section <a href=\"#sec:spheres\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:spheres\">4</a>, then choose $p_*$ sufficiently large, $\\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small, and the radius amplitude $R_*>0$ sufficiently small that $r_\\delta\\beta_\\delta^2\\le\\varepsilon$ for $0<\\delta\\le1$. For all sufficiently small spatial dilations there is a constant $C$, independent of the dilation and $\\delta$, such that $$\\label{ang:density-bound}\n \\|\\chi\\psi_\\delta\\|_{L^2(S_\\gamma Y)}\\le C$$ whenever $\\sum_{|\\alpha|\\le8}|\\partial^\\alpha h|\n\\le\\varepsilon r_\\delta$ on $Y$. Here $\\chi$ is the fixed cutoff from <a href=\"#sph:cutoff\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:cutoff\">[sph:cutoff]</a> and $\\psi_\\delta$ is the left-acting matrix density of Proposition <a href=\"#sph:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sph:transfer\">15</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-uniform-transfer-density",

      "method": "Subtract identity mass, localize the density equation, and apply system coercivity",

    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-mat-spanning",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-contact-coordinates"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-mat-spanning",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-mat-spanning</summary>\n\n*Proof.* As $\\lambda\\to0$, the coordinate columns converge smoothly to $y^l e_\\alpha$, $1\\le l\\le n$, $1\\le\\alpha\\le2$, where $e_\\alpha$ is the standard basis of $\\mathbb C^2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mat-spanning",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small $\\lambda$ and every $y\\in Y$, the vectors $$\\big(\\partial_i v_{1,\\nu}(y),\n             \\partial_i\\partial_j v_{1,\\nu}(y)\\big),\n \\qquad 1\\le\\nu\\le N,$$ span, over $\\mathbb C$, the space of pairs $(p,H)$ with $p_i\\in\\mathbb C^2$ and $H_{ij}=H_{ji}\\in\\mathbb C^2$ satisfying $$a^{ij}H_{ij}+B_1^ip_i=0.\n \\label{mat:jet-constraint}$$ The coefficients realizing prescribed such data may be chosen by a smooth right inverse, with uniform bounds to every fixed order.",
          "source_id": "PM-365.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-selected-jet-spanning",
      "inputs": [
        "Contact harmonic coordinate matrices",
        "Selected zero-value, zero-gradient harmonic columns",
        "Small spatial dilation"
      ],
      "method": "Affine and trace-free quadratic dilation limits with a smooth full-rank right inverse",
      "output": "Uniform realization of every admissible gradient and Hessian pair",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-mat-spanning"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-mat-moments",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-boundary-fixed-equivalence**: Equality of the same-patch boundary energy forms determines the smooth metric and unitary connection up to a diffeomorphism and unitary gauge fixing the measured patch.
- **invariant-cross-interpolation-margin**: Extension from the cross requires the stated interpolation inequalities and a strictly positive margin in the sum of their exponents. Uniform output estimates require uniform geometric, ellipticity and coefficient bounds.
- **invariant-transfer-reproduction**: The sphere transfer acts by a matrix density of total mass equal to the identity, maps local first-system harmonic inputs to second-system harmonic outputs, and reproduces paired harmonic functions agreeing on the known side.
- **invariant-conditional-density-control**: The localized transfer density has a uniform norm bound only under the stated profile choices and derivative comparison between the normalized principal tensors and the sphere radius.
- **invariant-finite-type-prolongation**: The conformal Killing principal part determines third derivatives from lower jets and derivatives of the forcing, with uniform coefficient bounds under uniform ellipticity and bounded smooth backgrounds.
- **invariant-exact-local-identification**: Conformal agreement and equality of the normalized harmonic-frame first-order coefficients yield exact metric agreement and a unitary connection identification, which also matches every corresponding source evaluation.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-finite-probe-certification**: Certify continuum metric and connection recovery from agreement on a finite collection of boundary probes. -> Agreement on selected probes does not establish the universal equality of boundary energy forms required by the theorem.
- **anti-use-lower-dimensional-prolongation**: Apply the finite-type displacement argument without the stated dimension restriction. -> The proof of injectivity of the prolonged principal symbol uses the dimension restriction explicitly; the result does not establish the same prolongation conclusion outside that regime.
- **anti-use-rough-coefficient-recovery**: Transfer the joint uniqueness conclusion directly to nonsmooth metric or connection coefficients. -> Boundary jet recovery, differentiated displacement equations and smooth prolongation are established for smooth coefficients; the paper supplies no rough-coefficient replacement.
- **anti-use-uniform-diagonal-kernel-bound**: Use fixed-separation kernel estimates as uniform bounds while sphere radii collapse. -> Fixed-separation constants may deteriorate near the diagonal. Existence of a continued kernel at every admissible positive radius does not itself bound the shrinking family.
- **anti-use-matrix-principal-commutators**: Reuse the scalar squared-norm commutator proof for systems with a general matrix-valued principal symbol. -> The scalar principal-symbol cancellations used in the perturbation estimate do not establish the analogous cancellations for noncommuting matrix principal symbols.
- **anti-use-unique-representative-recovery**: Interpret identical boundary measurements as equality of the original coefficient representatives in arbitrary coordinates and gauges. -> The theorem retains boundary-fixed diffeomorphism and unitary gauge ambiguities rather than identifying arbitrary representatives directly.
- **anti-use-higher-rank-extrapolation**: Apply the joint recovery theorem to arbitrary Hermitian bundle ranks or nontrivial bundles. -> The main result is formulated for the fixed trivial Hermitian rank-two bundle. Statements about other recovery results in the introduction do not extend this theorem's scope.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The joint recovery result concerns smooth coefficient fields and equality of boundary forms for all smooth patch-supported inputs and observations. Its continuation proof uses an infinite singular expansion and a shrinking-radius limit, rather than a finite reconstruction or decision procedure. The source states: "On the fixed open set $K^\circ\cap\{t<0\}$, which contains the origin, $r_\delta\to0$ as $\delta\to0$." Finite-type prolongation controls finitely many jets within this analytic proof; it does not turn the full continuum uniqueness statement into a finite executable oracle. A finite discretization would require additional approximation and certification contracts absent from the supplied paper.

The harmonic-frame convention <a href=\"#bdy:harmonic-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bdy:harmonic-normalization\">[bdy:harmonic-normalization]</a>, with the multipliers $c_j$ in <a href=\"#sph:frame-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sph:frame-normalization\">[sph:frame-normalization]</a>, gives equations whose second-derivative coefficient matrices are positive definite: $$\\label{ang:frame-equations}\n \\mathcal L_1=a^{ij}\\partial_i\\partial_j+B_1^i\\partial_i,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal L_2=Q^{ij}\\partial_i\\partial_j+B_2^i\\partial_i.$$ The $B_j^i$ are complex $2\\times2$ matrices acting on columns from the left. There is no zeroth-order term because each column of the defining frame $F_j$ is harmonic. All coefficients have uniform smooth bounds on the fixed coordinate neighborhood for sufficiently small spatial dilations. Suppress the dilation and shrinking parameters for now, and write $$r=r_\\delta,\\qquad f_*=\\log r,\\qquad\n \\beta=|df_*|_\\gamma,\\qquad f_i=\\nabla_i f_*.$$ The profile bounds supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#sph:profiles\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sph:profiles\">11</a> have the form $$\\label{ang:profile-bounds}\n \\beta\\ge\\beta_0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{Hess}_\\gamma f_*\\le-c_0\\beta\\gamma,\\qquad\n |\\nabla^j f_*|\\le C_j\\beta^j\\quad(1\\le j\\le8).$$ The lower threshold $\\beta_0$ can be increased by increasing $p_*$. Throughout this section we impose $$\\label{ang:smallness}\n \\sum_{|\\alpha|\\le8}|\\partial_y^\\alpha h|\\le\\varepsilon r,\n \\qquad r\\beta^2\\le\\varepsilon.$$ The first is the bootstrap assumption of Section <a href=\"#sec:spheres\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:spheres\">4</a>; the second is ensured by the choice of radius amplitude.",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The boundary determination theorem supplies an identifiability contract for smooth geometric coefficients, but no finite representation of the complete measured form or executable reconstruction procedure. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sphere family supports analytic continuation of harmonic sections; it does not specify a simulation update rule or a discrete approximation with certified error. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Recovery of boundary Taylor jets uses equality of full localized operator symbols. A finite measurement or inspection tool satisfying that premise is ABSENT. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A finite algebraic subconstruction is explicit: a full row rank matrix of selected admissible harmonic jets admits the right inverse T^*(TT^*)^{-1}. This can be adapted to supplied jet matrices, although obtaining the harmonic solutions from boundary measurements remains an analytic prerequisite. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Source evaluations identify points and fibers through a common space of smooth sources, but the paper does not give a finite storage representation or data structure for those evaluation maps. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The angular coercivity argument operates on smooth sphere functions and their differential operators. It provides no finite computational workload or arithmetic contract for accelerator execution. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The unitary gauge action is a symmetry of the connection equation and measured form. No cryptographic construction or security guarantee is supplied. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniform density bound is conditional on smooth coefficient and radius comparisons. It supplies no learning procedure or guarantee for estimated coefficients obtained from training data. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The exterior-cap argument characterizes a Dirichlet solution as a unique minimizer over a continuous function space. It gives no finite optimization formulation, stopping criterion, or convergence rate for computing that minimizer. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Agreement of matches on overlaps follows from source-evaluation surjectivity and unique continuation. This mathematical gluing result supplies no distributed protocol for coordinating independently computed states. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The direct scope is smooth geometric inverse boundary problems. Global identification is established through analytic continuation and boundary completion, rather than a finite engineering reconstruction contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-smooth-compact-connected-geometry** (hypothesis): The main recovery statement assumes a compact connected smooth manifold with nonempty smooth boundary and the stated dimension threshold. Other geometric or regularity regimes are not established here. Blocked use: ['Apply the main theorem to nonsmooth coefficient fields, noncompact domains, or disconnected interiors without a separate result.']
- **limit-fixed-trivial-bundle** (formal_scope): Joint recovery is formulated for the trivial Hermitian rank-two bundle and smooth unitary connections. The introduction's discussion of other connection results does not enlarge this theorem's bundle class. Blocked use: ['Assert joint recovery for arbitrary bundle rank, nontrivial bundle topology, or nonunitary connections.']
- **limit-complete-same-patch-data** (hypothesis): The measurement premise is exact equality of the zero-frequency energy forms for every smooth patch-supported input and observation. Agreement on finitely selected probes does not establish that premise. Blocked use: ['Certify the continuum uniqueness conclusion from finitely sampled boundary pairings.', 'Transfer the result to a different frequency or measurement operator without justification.']
- **limit-equivalence-class-recovery** (formal_scope): The conclusion retains a diffeomorphism and unitary gauge that fix the measured patch. It does not identify arbitrary original coefficient representatives directly. Blocked use: ['Conclude equality of the original metrics and connection matrices in arbitrary coordinates and gauges.']
- **limit-boundary-jets-not-collar-equality** (edge_case): Boundary jet equality provides smooth exterior pasting, rather than equality of the coefficient fields on an interior collar. Blocked use: ['Skip exterior-cap construction by asserting interior agreement from equal boundary Taylor jets.']
- **limit-source-free-harmonic-jets** (hypothesis): The harmonic second-jet characterization applies away from the closure of the source set. Boundary evaluations vanish, so the interior fiber-surjectivity assertion cannot be transferred to boundary points. Blocked use: ['Prescribe arbitrary harmonic second jets at a point inside the source region using this lemma.', 'Use boundary evaluations as surjective fiber maps.']
- **limit-source-realization-not-finite-reconstruction** (nonconstructive): Distributional transposition, unique continuation, and duality establish source-value and jet realization without supplying a finite procedure for finding the required sources. Blocked use: ['Treat existence of suitable source solutions as an executable reconstruction contract from sampled measurements.']
- **limit-separated-scalar-principal-systems** (hypothesis): Product continuation uses equations acting on separate tensor indices, with each coefficient depending only on its indicated variable and with scalar real elliptic principal part. Blocked use: ['Apply the product-continuation argument unchanged to coupled variable dependence or a general matrix principal symbol.']
- **limit-cross-interpolation-margin** (hypothesis): Extension from the cross requires compatible data and the stated factor interpolation inequalities. Uniform estimates additionally require uniform geometric margins, ellipticity, coefficient bounds, and a positive interpolation-exponent margin. Blocked use: ['Infer cross extension from compatibility alone.', 'Retain uniform estimates while the required margins degenerate.']
- **limit-infinite-series-continuation** (nonconstructive): The cross-extension proof constructs its solution using a singular-system series and elliptic regularity. A finite truncation certificate for exact continuation is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Interpret a truncated singular expansion as certified exact continuation without an additional remainder bound.']
- **limit-strict-product-surface-test** (hypothesis): Local product extension requires nonzero partial gradients and strict positivity of the product Hessian test. The perturbation statement preserves these strict hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Continue across a degenerate surface using this criterion.', 'Replace strict positivity by a nonnegative test without another argument.']
- **limit-cylinder-seed-and-overlap-geometry** (hypothesis): Cylinder propagation requires a compact base, top and lateral seed neighborhoods, upward closure of the seed, and strict extension criteria on the remaining levels. Overlap agreement is proved through vertical paths to previously identified data. Blocked use: ['Glue continuation germs merely because their output neighborhoods cover a surface.', 'Discard the upward-closed seed condition.']
- **limit-fixed-separation-initialization** (edge_case): Initialization bounds are uniform in the spatial dilation with fixed location and positive-separation parameters. They do not establish a uniform kernel bound as separation collapses. Blocked use: ['Use fixed-separation initialization as the bound controlling the diagonal limit.']
- **limit-ordered-parameter-choices** (hypothesis): The reference geometry, profile exponent, smallness parameter, radius amplitude, and fixed-separation ranges are chosen before the final spatial dilation. The shrinking parameter remains subject to the provisional comparison. Blocked use: ['Choose a different spatial dilation for every shrinking radius and claim the fixed-dilation conclusion.', 'Ignore dependencies among geometry, smallness, and initialization scales.']
- **limit-positive-radius-existence** (edge_case): Sphere-family continuation gives smoothness for each admissible positive shrinking parameter. Existence alone does not supply a bound for the continued kernel in the shrinking limit. Blocked use: ['Infer uniform shrinking-family kernel bounds solely from continuation to each positive-radius sphere.']
- **limit-harmonic-transfer-inputs** (hypothesis): The transfer's local harmonicity assertion concerns first harmonic-frame inputs defined near the closed ball. Reproduction of a corresponding pair additionally uses agreement on the known side and the stated fixed neighborhoods. Blocked use: ['Apply harmonic reproduction to arbitrary nonharmonic inputs.', 'Assert pair reproduction without the known-side agreement.']
- **limit-coercivity-mean-zero-and-smallness** (hypothesis): Angular coercivity applies to smooth functions compactly supported in the base and with zero fiber mean, under the profile and tensor-smallness assumptions. The matrix estimate follows from scalar coercivity and an ordered lower-order comparison. Blocked use: ['Apply the coercivity estimate directly to a density with nonzero identity mass.', 'Replace the scalar principal tensors by noncommuting matrix principal coefficients.']
- **limit-density-bound-is-conditional** (hypothesis): The localized density estimate requires the ordered profile choices, radius-weight smallness, and the provisional derivative comparison between the normalized principal tensors and the radius. Cutoff-region control is a separate input. Blocked use: ['Claim the uniform density bound without proving its derivative comparison.', 'Omit the cutoff-region bounds when applying coercivity to the localized density.']
- **limit-selected-jet-right-inverse** (formal_scope): The finite right-inverse construction assumes a supplied selected harmonic family, admissible jet data, full row rank, and the uniform rank control obtained at sufficiently small spatial dilation. It is a local algebraic subconstruction rather than complete boundary reconstruction. Blocked use: ['Invert a rank-deficient selected-jet matrix.', 'Infer uniform family bounds without a lower bound on its nonzero singular values.', 'Treat the right inverse as an algorithm producing the required harmonic solutions from boundary measurements.']
- **limit-moment-interpolation-and-core** (hypothesis): Weighted moment estimates assume the provisional tensor comparison. Their pointwise derivative consequences also require the fixed interpolation choices, uniform smooth bounds, membership in the core, and a radius below the stated threshold. Blocked use: ['Use the pointwise moment bounds outside the core or above the small-radius threshold.', 'Differentiate the moment bounds without the interpolation argument and its regularity assumptions.']
- **limit-finite-type-dimension-and-background** (hypothesis): The finite-type lemma assumes the stated dimension restriction, a smooth positive definite principal tensor, and a smooth real-linear zeroth-order perturbation. Uniform coefficient bounds require uniform ellipticity and bounded smooth backgrounds. Blocked use: ['Apply the prolongation conclusion outside the stated dimension regime.', 'Retain uniform prolongation bounds as ellipticity degenerates.', 'Replace the zeroth-order perturbation by an unspecified differential coupling.']
- **limit-bootstrap-collapse-not-finite-oracle** (nonconstructive): Local operator equality is obtained by preserving the provisional comparison over the connected shrinking-parameter interval and then taking a radius-collapse limit with a fixed spatial dilation. A finite decision certificate for the full continuum statement is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Promote agreement at selected positive radii to exact coefficient equality near the contact point.']
- **limit-conformal-removal-without-potential** (formal_scope): Exact removal of the conformal factor uses equal normalized harmonic-frame first-order coefficients, vanishing zeroth-order terms, and agreement on the known open side of a connected neighborhood. An additional matrix potential is outside this argument's established scope. Blocked use: ['Conclude exact metric agreement from conformal agreement alone.', 'Extend the conformal-removal argument to an additional potential without a separate proof.']
- **limit-global-and-boundary-completion** (hypothesis): Global identification uses maximal-match continuation, compactness, source evaluations, and connected interiors. Smooth boundary completion uses normal flows and connection transport; identity on the entire measured patch is recovered using arbitrary patch-supported boundary traces. Blocked use: ['Infer smooth boundary extension from a local match alone.', 'Infer identity on every measured component solely from identity on the attachment patch.']
- **limit-no-noise-or-runtime-contract** (unknown): A noise-dependent stability estimate, finite-data reconstruction method, and certified runtime for the full inverse problem are ABSENT from the supplied paper. Blocked use: ['Claim stable numerical recovery from noisy boundary samples.', 'Attach a computational complexity guarantee to the full uniqueness theorem.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that zero-frequency boundary measurements on any nonempty open boundary patch determine both a smooth Riemannian metric and a smooth unitary connection on the trivial Hermitian rank-two bundle over a compact connected smooth manifold of dimension at least three with smooth boundary. Both inputs and observations are restricted to the same patch. The metric and connection are determined up to a diffeomorphism and a unitary gauge that restrict to the identity on the measured patch.

<a id="sec:intro"></a>
### Introduction

A connection Laplacian couples the geometry of a manifold to parallel transport in a vector bundle. Its boundary measurements therefore pose two simultaneous inverse problems: determining the metric that governs its principal part and determining the connection that governs the transport of vector-valued solutions. We consider both unknowns at zero frequency, with all measurements confined to one boundary patch.

<a id="eq:DN"></a>
Let $M$ be a compact connected smooth manifold of dimension $n\geq3$, with nonempty smooth boundary, and let $\Gamma\subset\partial M$ be a nonempty relatively open proper subset. The bundle is the trivial Hermitian bundle $M\times\mathbb C^2$. A smooth unitary connection is written $d_A=d+A$, where $$A\in C^\infty(M;T^*M\otimes\mathfrak u(2)),\qquad
 \mathfrak u(2)=\{B\in\mathbb C^{2\times2}:B^*=-B\}.$$ For a smooth Riemannian metric $g$, write $L_{g,A}=d_A^{*g}d_A$. Its zero-Dirichlet realization is invertible: a section of zero energy is parallel, and a parallel section with zero boundary trace is zero. Elliptic coercivity then gives, for each $f\in C_c^\infty(\Gamma;\mathbb C^2)$ extended by zero to $\partial M$, a unique solution $u_f^{g,A}$ of $$L_{g,A}u_f^{g,A}=0\quad\text{in }M,\qquad
 u_f^{g,A}|_{\partial M}=f.$$ The measured object is the sesquilinear energy form $$\label{eq:DN}
 \langle\Lambda_{g,A,\Gamma}f,h\rangle
 =\int_M\langle d_Au_f^{g,A},d_Au_h^{g,A}\rangle_g\,dV_g,
 \qquad f,h\in C_c^\infty(\Gamma;\mathbb C^2).$$ The Hermitian product is linear in its first argument. All integrations use densities; no orientation of $M$ is required. In particular, <a href="#eq:DN" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:DN">[eq:DN]</a> specifies the boundary measurement without presupposing a boundary metric or a boundary measure.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main-conclusion"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *Let $M$ and $\Gamma$ be as above. For $j=1,2$, let $g_j$ be a smooth Riemannian metric on $M$ and let $A_j\in C^\infty(M;T^*M\otimes\mathfrak u(2))$. If $$\langle\Lambda_{g_1,A_1,\Gamma}f,h\rangle
 =\langle\Lambda_{g_2,A_2,\Gamma}f,h\rangle
 \quad\text{for all }f,h\in C_c^\infty(\Gamma;\mathbb C^2),$$ then there are a smooth diffeomorphism $\Phi:M\longrightarrow M$ and a smooth map $U:M\longrightarrow U(2)$ such that $$\label{eq:main-conclusion}
 \Phi|_\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{Id}},\qquad U|_\Gamma=I,\qquad
 g_2=\Phi^*g_1,\qquad
 A_2=U^{-1}(\Phi^*A_1)U+U^{-1}dU.$$ Here $I$ denotes the $2\times2$ identity matrix.*

</div>

The two transformations in <a href="#eq:main-conclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main-conclusion">[eq:main-conclusion]</a> preserve <a href="#eq:DN" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:DN">[eq:DN]</a>: the corresponding change of solution is $u_2=U^{-1}(u_1\circ\Phi)$. Thus the conclusion describes precisely the ambiguities inherent in these measurements. The patch need not be connected, and the manifold may have several boundary components.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and contribution

Calderón’s inverse conductivity problem asks whether electrical measurements at the boundary determine an interior conductivity (Calderón 1980) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Calderon1980](#ref-Calderon1980)]. For smooth isotropic conductivities in dimension at least three, global uniqueness was established by Sylvester and Uhlmann (Sylvester and Uhlmann 1987) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[SylvesterUhlmann1987](#ref-SylvesterUhlmann1987)]. In the anisotropic formulation the unknown is a metric, or equivalently a positive conductivity tensor density, and boundary-fixing diffeomorphisms give an unavoidable invariance. Lee and Uhlmann proved a boundary determination result and uniqueness in the real-analytic setting under geometric and topological hypotheses (Lee and Uhlmann 1989) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LeeUhlmann1989](#ref-LeeUhlmann1989)]. Lassas and Uhlmann subsequently recovered real-analytic manifolds from local boundary information (Lassas and Uhlmann 2001) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[LassasUhlmann2001](#ref-LassasUhlmann2001)]. The Poisson embedding approach of Lassas, Liimatainen, and Salo (Lassas, Liimatainen, and Salo 2020) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[LLS20](#ref-LLS20)] expresses the geometry through the values of harmonic solutions. We use the same broad principle, with source solutions of a connection Laplacian providing both points and fiber identifications.

For connections, Albin, Guillarmou, Tzou, and Uhlmann recovered Hermitian connections and matrix potentials from full Cauchy data on fixed surfaces (Albin et al. 2013) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[AlbinGuillarmouTzouUhlmann2013](#ref-AlbinGuillarmouTzouUhlmann2013)]. In higher dimensions, Cekić established recovery for line bundles on certain known conformally transversally anisotropic geometries (Cekić 2017) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Cekic2017Connections](#ref-Cekic2017Connections)], and for smooth unitary Yang–Mills connections of arbitrary rank from same-patch measurements on an arbitrary fixed smooth metric (Cekić 2020, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Cekic2020YM](#ref-Cekic2020YM)]. His complex parallel transport method also recovers arbitrary-rank connections and matrix potentials from full boundary data on fixed geometries conformal to subdomains of $\mathbb R^2\times M_0$ (Cekić 2025, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Cekic2025Systems](#ref-Cekic2025Systems)]. In the real-analytic category, Gabdurakhmanov and Kokarev proved joint recovery of metric, Euclidean bundle, and compatible connection from a boundary patch (Gabdurakhmanov and Kokarev 2025, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[GabdurakhmanovKokarev2025](#ref-GabdurakhmanovKokarev2025)]. Their use of Green kernels to identify the base and fibers is a close geometric antecedent. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> concerns an unknown smooth metric and an arbitrary smooth unitary connection on the fixed trivial rank-two bundle; it imposes no Yang–Mills equation on that connection.

The scalar companion (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[ScalarCompanion2026](#ref-ScalarCompanion2026)] develops a smooth continuation argument based on separately harmonic Green kernels and shrinking geodesic spheres. The present proof uses its product continuation, sphere geometry, and scalar angular estimate (OpenAI 2026, secs. 3–5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ScalarCompanion2026](#ref-ScalarCompanion2026)]. We give the analytic arguments needed here, including their extension to systems with scalar principal part. The scalar uniqueness theorem itself is not applied to the matrix-valued boundary data.

The additional difficulty appears in the first moments of the transfer between local solution spaces. In scalar harmonic coordinates those moments can be normalized away. For a connection, a harmonic frame removes the zeroth-order term, but the transfer still has a matrix-valued first moment. We represent it by matrices $D^1,\ldots,D^n$. Their components complementary to the real scalar matrices satisfy a first-order system whose principal symbol is the conformal Killing symbol. In dimension at least three this symbol is of finite type: two prolongations determine all third derivatives from lower jets. The real scalar part of the displacement is fixed by the coordinate normalization. Together these facts turn an initial square-root estimate on $D$ into the stronger estimate needed to continue the metric and connection across a frontier point. This reduction is the main matrix-specific step of the proof.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### How the proof is organized

Boundary symbol factorization first determines all metric and connection jets on $\Gamma$, after imposing normal gauge. Attaching a small exterior cap then converts the boundary form into equality of Green matrices on an open interior set. Source solutions supported in that set separate points, span fiber values, and realize the allowed harmonic jets. They define a maximal local isometry with a unitary bundle identification; Section <a href="#sec:boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:boundary">[sec:boundary]</a> prepares coordinates and harmonic frames at any putative frontier point.

The local continuation mechanism has three stages. First, Section <a href="#sec:product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:product">[sec:product]</a> continues a mixed Green matrix, initially known when either variable lies in the matched region. Two quantitative unique-continuation estimates, one in each variable, allow continuation across suitable hypersurfaces in the product. Second, Sections <a href="#sec:spheres" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:spheres">4</a> and <a href="#sec:angular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:angular">[sec:angular]</a> use this kernel to transfer harmonic functions across small moving spheres. The transfer is represented by a matrix density of total mass $I$. An angular coercivity estimate bounds that density uniformly even as the spheres shrink. The matrix lower-order terms are absorbed in the same estimate.

Finally, Section <a href="#sec:matrix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:matrix">6</a> extracts the first and second moments of the transfer. The finite-type argument described above improves the difference of the normalized inverse metrics from order $r$ to order $r^{3/2}$, where $r$ is the sphere radius. A continuity argument then lets the radii collapse, proving local agreement of the normalized operators. Section <a href="#sec:global" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:global">[sec:global]</a> removes the remaining conformal factor and extends the resulting isometry and unitary identification over the whole manifold, including every component of the measured patch.

<a id="bdy:section"></a>
### Boundary data, exterior sources, and contact coordinates

<span id="sec:boundary" label="sec:boundary"></span>

We first replace the measurements by a family of interior source solutions. The boundary symbol determines jets of both the metric and the connection, after a boundary-identity change of gauge. These jets allow a common exterior cap, on which the Green matrices agree. Source evaluations in that cap then identify points and fibers simultaneously. The final part of the section prepares coordinates and harmonic frames at a possible frontier of this identification. The cap and source-evaluation construction follows the scalar construction in (OpenAI 2026, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ScalarCompanion2026](#ref-ScalarCompanion2026)]; we give the changes needed for unitary connections and matrix-valued evaluations.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Dirichlet solutions and boundary jets

We use positive metric densities throughout. Write $L_{g,A}=d_A^{*g}d_A$, with positive principal symbol, and let $L_{g,A,D}$ denote its zero-Dirichlet realization. Its quadratic form is coercive on $H^1_0(M;\mathbb C^2)$. Indeed, the usual elliptic form estimate and compactness reduce coercivity to the absence of a kernel. A kernel element satisfies $d_Au=0$; its norm is constant, and its zero boundary trace makes it zero. Smooth sources and boundary data therefore have unique solutions smooth up to the boundary. The same facts hold on every smooth extension used below and on sufficiently small coordinate balls; we use the standard smooth elliptic Dirichlet theory of (Taylor 2011, vol. 115, chap. 5) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[TaylorPDEI2011](#ref-TaylorPDEI2011)].

We also use open-set unique continuation for systems with a smooth, real scalar elliptic principal part and smooth matrix lower-order terms; see (Aronszajn 1957, Remark 3) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Aronszajn1957](#ref-Aronszajn1957)]. The usual scalar local Carleman estimate, summed over components, proves this statement: after conjugation by the scalar weight, a matrix first-order term is bounded by a constant times $\|\nabla v\|+\tau\|v\|$, and is absorbed for large Carleman parameter $\tau$. See (Hörmander 2009, chap. XXVIII) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[HormanderIV2009](#ref-HormanderIV2009)] for the underlying scalar estimates. Boundary Cauchy uniqueness follows from the same interior statement. Extend the coefficients smoothly across a boundary patch and extend a solution with zero trace and zero covariant normal derivative by zero. Green’s formula leaves no boundary distribution, so the extension solves the equation weakly. Interior regularity and unique continuation then give vanishing near the patch and throughout the connected interior.

A unitary gauge $V:M\to U(2)$ changes the connection to <a id="bdy:gauge"></a>$$\label{bdy:gauge}
 A^V=V^{-1}AV+V^{-1}dV,\qquad
 d_{A^V}(V^{-1}u)=V^{-1}d_Au.$$ A gauge equal to $I$ on the boundary preserves the measured form. In inward boundary-normal coordinates $(z,s)$, solve $\partial_sV=-A_sV$, $V(z,0)=I$. This gives $A^V_s=0$. The gauge can be made global: in a collar replace the time argument of this solution by a smooth function equal to $s$ in a smaller collar and equal to zero near the inner edge, and set $V=I$ farther inside. The ODE preserves unitarity. Apply this construction separately to $A_1,A_2$, using gauges $V_1,V_2$, and retain their names for the final return to the original trivializations. Until then, $A_j$ denotes the gauged connection.

<a id="stmt-bdy-jets"></a>
<div id="bdy:jets" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Boundary jets). *Suppose the local energy forms of $(g_1,A_1)$ and $(g_2,A_2)$ agree on $\Gamma$. In their respective boundary-normal coordinates, based on the same boundary coordinates, and in the normal gauges just constructed, the full Taylor jets of $g_1,A_1$ and $g_2,A_2$ agree at every point of $\Gamma$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bdy-jets"><summary>Proof of stmt-bdy-jets</summary>

*Proof.* Write $g=ds^2+k(s,z)$, with $k$ a tangential positive matrix, and put $\rho=(k^{ab}\xi_a\xi_b)^{1/2}$. Green’s formula identifies the measured operator with the density $\nabla^A_\nu u\,dS_g$. Relative to the coordinate density, its principal symbol is $$(\det k)^{1/2}\rho I.$$ Its square determines $C=(\det k)k^{-1}$. Since the tangential dimension is $m=n-1$, $$\det C=(\det k)^{m-1},\qquad
 k=(\det C)^{1/(m-1)}C^{-1}.$$ Thus the boundary metrics and densities agree. Division by their common density gives equality of the ordinary covariant unit-normal DN operators locally on $\Gamma$. Localization on both sides by cutoffs supported in $\Gamma$ gives equality of their full symbols.

We spell out the symbol calculation, extending the metric factorization of (Lee and Uhlmann 1989) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[LeeUhlmann1989](#ref-LeeUhlmann1989)] to the present scalar-principal system. Recovery of a connection together with the metric by this even–odd symbol separation is also developed in (Gabdurakhmanov 2021, Theorem 1.1 and Section 3.3) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Gabdurakhmanov2021DN](#ref-Gabdurakhmanov2021DN)]; the calculation below uses complex Hermitian fibers. In normal gauge, $$L_{g,A}=-\partial_s^2-\alpha\partial_s+P,
 \qquad \alpha=\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(k^{-1}\partial_s k),$$ where $P$ is the connection Laplacian on each tangential slice. With $D_z=-i\partial_z$, its degree-one symbol consists of the scalar metric term and $-2ik^{ab}A_a\xi_b$. Its degree-zero symbol uses only slice coefficients and their tangential derivatives. The local Dirichlet factorization has the form $$L_{g,A}=(-\partial_s+B-\alpha)(\partial_s+B)
                 \pmod{\Psi^{-\infty}},
 \qquad
 b\mathbin{\#}b-\alpha b-\partial_s b\sim\sigma(P),$$ where $b\sim\rho I+b_0+b_{-1}+\cdots$ is a left symbol and $$b\mathbin{\#}c\sim
 \sum_{\mu}\frac{(1/i)^{|\mu|}}{\mu!}
       (\partial_\xi^\mu b)(\partial_z^\mu c).$$ The positive root $\rho I$ selects the decaying Dirichlet branch. The Poisson parametrix consequently identifies $B(0)$, modulo a smoothing operator, with the outward normal derivative. This factorization is valid for smooth coefficients: its principal root is scalar and each subsequent matrix coefficient is solved by scalar division by $2\rho$. The true inverse Dirichlet problem changes only the local smoothing remainder.

<a id="bdy:highest-jets"></a>
The part of $b_0$ involving the new jets is <a id="bdy:first-jets"></a>$$\label{bdy:first-jets}
 \frac14\left(\operatorname{tr}_k\partial_s k
       -\frac{(\partial_s k)(\xi^\sharp,\xi^\sharp)}{\rho^2}\right)I
       -\frac{ik^{ab}A_a\xi_b}{\rho},
 \qquad \xi^\sharp=k^{-1}\xi.$$ This follows from $\partial_s\rho=-(\partial_s k)(\xi^\sharp,\xi^\sharp)/(2\rho)$ and the degree-one factorization equation. At degree $1-j$, $j\ge2$, the new normal derivatives arise only in $\partial_s b_{2-j}/(2\rho)$. Inductively their contribution is $$\label{bdy:highest-jets}
 \frac{1}{(2\rho)^{j-1}}
 \left[
 \frac14\left(\operatorname{tr}_k\partial_s^j k
       -\frac{(\partial_s^j k)(\xi^\sharp,\xi^\sharp)}{\rho^2}\right)I
       -\frac{ik^{ab}(\partial_s^{j-1}A_a)\xi_b}{\rho}
 \right].$$ Every omitted term involves lower normal jets, with tangential derivatives. Differentiating $\rho$, a raised index, or a coefficient multiplying the preceding highest jet produces only such lower-jet expressions.

Subtract the two symbol recursions after their lower jets have been identified. The metric term in <a href="#bdy:highest-jets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bdy:highest-jets">[bdy:highest-jets]</a> is even in $\xi$, whereas the connection term is odd. The odd term determines all components of the new connection jet. If the even term vanishes for a symmetric tensor $T$, then $T(v,v)=\operatorname{tr}_k(T)$ for every unit vector $v$. Polarization gives $T=\operatorname{tr}_k(T)k$, and taking trace gives $(m-1)\operatorname{tr}_k(T)=0$. As $m\ge2$, $T=0$. Beginning with the recovered boundary metric, this proves all normal jets by induction; their tangential derivatives recover all mixed jets. The remaining normal metric components and normal connection component are fixed by the coordinate and gauge choices. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### A common cap and its Green matrices

Jet equality does not assert equality in an interior collar. It provides instead a common smooth extension on the exterior side of a smaller patch. The following variational argument turns that exterior region into common interior data.

<a id="stmt-bdy-cap"></a>
<div id="bdy:cap" class="proposition">

<a id="bdy:common-green"></a>
**Proposition 3** (Common exterior sources). *There are compact connected smooth extensions $N_j$ of the original manifold, with nonempty smooth boundary, and a common connected open exterior cap $E\subset N_j^\circ$, attached through a nonempty open patch $P\Subset\Gamma$, such that the extended metrics and connections agree on $E$. For each $f\in C_c^\infty(E;\mathbb C^2)$, the solutions $L_{j,D}^{-1}f$ agree on $E$. The Dirichlet Green matrices, normalized using metric volume, satisfy $$\label{bdy:common-green}
 G_1(x,y)=G_2(x,y)\quad(x,y\in E,\ x\ne y),
 \qquad G_j(x,y)=G_j(y,x)^*.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bdy-cap"><summary>Proof of stmt-bdy-cap</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a boundary chart compactly contained in $\Gamma$, and a nonnegative smooth bump $b$ whose support is compact in that chart and whose positivity set $P=\{b>0\}$ is nonempty and connected. Using the separate normal collars, move the boundary from $s=0$ to the graph $s=-b(z)$, with $b$ small enough to remain inside an extended collar. The common open cap is $$E=\{(z,s):-b(z)<s<0\}.$$ Extend the first coefficient fields smoothly to negative $s$, retaining positive definiteness of the metric and skew-Hermitian values of the connection. Use the same exterior extension for the second pair. Lemma <a href="#bdy:jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bdy:jets">2</a> makes the resulting pasting smooth to all orders on a neighborhood of $\operatorname{supp}b$. Restricting it to $s\ge-b(z)$ gives smoothness also at the flat edges of the bump. The bundles remain trivial in the extended normal gauges.

For the energy comparison only, identify the extensions by the identity on the original $M$ and by cap coordinates on $E$. In collar charts this map and its inverse are continuous and piecewise smooth with bounded first derivatives, and hence bi-Lipschitz. They identify the relevant $H^1_0$ spaces. Fix a vector source $f$ compactly supported in $E$. The solution $w_j=L_{j,D}^{-1}f$ is the unique minimizer of $$\mathcal J_j(w)=\frac12\int_{N_j}|d_{A_j}w|_{g_j}^2\,dV_{g_j}
       -\operatorname{Re}\int_E\langle f,w\rangle\,dV_{g_j}
       \quad\text{on }H^1_0(N_j;\mathbb C^2).$$ Its trace on the original boundary is smooth and supported in $\operatorname{supp}b\Subset\Gamma$, because the original and extended boundaries coincide wherever $b=0$. Transport $w_1$ to $N_2$, and inside the original $M$ replace it by the second harmonic extension of this trace. The replacing difference has zero trace on $\partial M$, so its zero extension is in $H^1_0(N_2)$. The competitor is therefore admissible even at the narrowing edges of the cap.

On $M$, the source is zero and $w_1$ is the first harmonic extension of its trace. Equality of the measured quadratic forms makes its interior energy equal to that of the replacement. On $E$, both coefficients and the source pairing are unchanged. Thus $\min\mathcal J_2\le\min\mathcal J_1$. Reversing the argument gives equality, and uniqueness of the minimizer proves $w_1=w_2$ on $E$.

Normalize the Green matrix by $$L_{j,x}G_j(x,y)=\delta_y^{g_j}(x)I,\qquad
 G_j(\cdot,y)|_{\partial N_j}=0,\qquad
 L_{j,D}^{-1}f(x)=\int_{N_j}G_j(x,y)f(y)\,dV_{g_j}(y).$$ The source densities on $E$ coincide. Varying the source gives equality of the distribution kernels on $E\times E$, hence pointwise equality off the diagonal. Self-adjointness of the Dirichlet inverse gives the adjoint symmetry in <a href="#bdy:common-green" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bdy:common-green">[bdy:common-green]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Evaluations identify points and fibers

Fix a nonempty open set $U_0\Subset E$. For $f\in C_c^\infty(U_0;\mathbb C^2)$, set <a id="bdy:source-evaluations"></a>$$\label{bdy:source-evaluations}
 w_{j,f}=L_{j,D}^{-1}f,\qquad I_j(p)f=w_{j,f}(p).$$ Thus $I_j(p)$ is a linear map from the common source space to the fiber at $p$. The maps agree on $E$. This construction is related to the source and Poisson embeddings of (Lassas, Liimatainen, and Salo 2020, sec. 6.1 and Appendix A) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[LLS20](#ref-LLS20)]. The following point-distribution proof, in the spirit of (Malgrange 1956, chap. III, Section 3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Malgrange1956](#ref-Malgrange1956)], supplies the precise system-valued separation needed here.

<a id="stmt-bdy-evaluations"></a>
<div id="bdy:evaluations" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Values and harmonic two-jets). *Let $N$ be a compact connected smooth manifold of dimension $n\ge2$ with nonempty boundary, let $L=d_A^{*g}d_A$, and let $U\Subset N^\circ$ be a nonempty open source set.*

1.  *At each interior point, $f\mapsto L_D^{-1}f(p)$ is onto $\mathbb C^2$. At any two distinct interior points the two values are jointly onto $\mathbb C^2\oplus\mathbb C^2$.*

2.  *If $p\notin\overline U$, the second jets of $L_D^{-1}f$ at $p$ fill exactly the linear space of jets satisfying $Lu(p)=0$.*

3.  *Each evaluation is zero on $\partial N$, and for fixed $f$ varies continuously up to that boundary.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bdy-evaluations"><summary>Proof of stmt-bdy-evaluations</summary>

*Proof.* The last assertion is smooth Dirichlet regularity. For the first two, a complex-linear functional on values or jets is represented by a dual-vector distribution $T$, supported at one or two points. Suppose it annihilates all source solutions, and define $v=(L_D^{-1})^tT$ by distributional transposition. Then $$\langle v,f\rangle=\langle T,L_D^{-1}f\rangle=0
                    \quad(f\in C_c^\infty(U;\mathbb C^2)),
 \qquad L^tv=T.$$ Here the transpose and pairing are bilinear on the dual bundle; no complex conjugation convention is needed. Elliptic regularity makes $v$ smooth off the support of $T$. The interior with one or two points removed is connected: a path can be diverted around each point in a punctured coordinate ball when $n\ge2$. Unique continuation from the nonempty set obtained by removing these points from $U$ makes $v$ supported at those points in the interior. If a point belongs to $U$, the asserted initial vanishing is distributional there as well.

At each support point a distribution is a finite sum of derivatives of the point mass. If its highest derivative order is $k$, its image under $L^t$ has exact highest order $k+2$. Indeed, the highest homogeneous coefficient vector polynomial is multiplied by the scalar elliptic quadratic polynomial; this multiplication is injective. Consequently no nonzero order-zero distribution can be the image of a point-supported distribution. There are therefore no value annihilators, proving both surjectivity claims.

For a two-jet annihilator at $p\notin\overline U$, the image $T$ has order at most two. Thus $v=c\delta_p$, with $c$ a dual-fiber vector. Its image is precisely the functional $u\mapsto c(Lu(p))$. Conversely all such functionals annihilate the source solutions. Finite-dimensional duality gives the exact stated jet space. In particular, values and gradients may be prescribed freely, since the Hessian trace can be chosen to satisfy the equation. ◻

</details>

<a id="bdy:matching"></a>
We can now define the correspondence whose continuation will prove the theorem. A *match* consists of a local isometry $\Phi:W\to N_1^\circ$, with $W\subset N_2^\circ$ connected, open, and containing $E$, and a smooth unitary bundle map $J$ from the second fiber at $p$ to the first fiber at $\Phi(p)$. We require $$\label{bdy:matching}
 \Phi|_E=\operatorname{Id},\qquad J|_E=I,\qquad
 d_{\Phi^*A_1}(Ju)=Jd_{A_2}u,\qquad
 J(p)I_2(p)=I_1(\Phi(p)).$$ In the connection identity the first connection is pulled back to $W$. The identity on $E$ is a match.

<a id="stmt-bdy-matches"></a>
<div id="bdy:matches" class="lemma">

<a id="bdy:matched-green"></a>
**Lemma 5** (The maximal match). *All matches agree on overlaps and are injective on base points. Their union is a unique maximal match $(\Phi,J)$ on a connected open set $W\subset N_2^\circ$. On this set, $$\label{bdy:matched-green}
 G_1(\Phi(p),\Phi(q))
       =J(p)G_2(p,q)J(q)^*\qquad(p,q\in W,\ p\ne q).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bdy-matches"><summary>Proof of stmt-bdy-matches</summary>

*Proof.* If two possible images of the same point were distinct, their evaluation identities would impose a fixed invertible linear relation between the evaluations at two distinct points of $N_1^\circ$. This contradicts joint surjectivity in Lemma <a href="#bdy:evaluations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bdy:evaluations">4</a>. Once the image agrees, surjectivity at the source point determines $J$ uniquely. The same argument using the evaluations in $N_2$ rules out two base points with the same image. Thus the maps glue on every overlap. Their union is connected because every domain contains $E$, and retains all the properties in <a href="#bdy:matching" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bdy:matching">[bdy:matching]</a>.

For fixed $p$, the evaluation identity and the common source density give <a href="#bdy:matched-green" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bdy:matched-green">[bdy:matched-green]</a> when $q\in U_0$, away from the pole. As a function of $q$, the adjoint of each side solves the same pulled-back connection equation after the bundle identification. Injectivity of $\Phi$ ensures that $q=p$ is the only possible pole. Unique continuation on the connected set $W\setminus\{p\}$ proves the identity everywhere claimed. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Coordinates at a possible interior frontier

It remains to show that the maximal domain is the entire interior. We next prepare the local data needed to enlarge it. The use of harmonic *frames* is essential: constant coefficient columns in these frames solve the equation, even when the connection has no nonzero parallel sections.

<a id="stmt-bdy-contact"></a>
<div id="bdy:contact" class="proposition">

**Proposition 6** (Contact data). *If the maximal domain $W$ has an interior frontier, there are a point $p\in\partial W\cap N_2^\circ$, a point $q\in N_1^\circ$, and charts about $p,q$, both with coordinate range a neighborhood of $0\in\mathbb R^n$, with the following properties.*

1.  *Both charts avoid $\overline{U_0}$. The old match is coordinate identity on its domain in these charts. That domain contains a region $x_n<\kappa(x')$, where $\kappa$ is smooth, $\kappa(0)=0$, and [(bdy:limit-match)](#bdy:limit-match)$d\kappa(0)=0$.*

2.  *In smooth local unitary frames, the metrics, connection matrices, and Green matrices agree on the known region (for the Green matrices, on its square off the diagonal), and $g_1(0)=g_2(0)=I_n$.*

<a id="bdy:trace-coordinates"></a>
3.  *There are invertible matrix functions $F_j$ with harmonic columns, chosen as corresponding source solutions. In these harmonic frames there are corresponding harmonic matrix functions $X_j^l$, $1\le l\le n$, satisfying $$\label{bdy:trace-coordinates}
     \tfrac12\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{tr}X_j^l(x)=x^l,
     \qquad X_j^l(0)=0,
     \qquad \partial_iX_j^l(0)=\delta_i^l I.$$*

4.  *There are finitely many further corresponding harmonic column functions with zero value and gradient at $0$, whose Hessians at $0$ span the space of symmetric $\mathbb C^2$-valued tensors $H$ satisfying $\sum_i H_{ii}=0$. All these paired functions, including the frames, agree on the known region and have identical jets at $0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bdy-contact"><summary>Proof of stmt-bdy-contact</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a small coordinate ball near an interior frontier and a point of $W$ close enough to that frontier that its distance to the complement is attained inside the chart. The Euclidean ball with that center and radius lies in $W$ and touches its complement at an interior point $p$. Its boundary gives the required smooth one-sided contact hypersurface.

<a id="bdy:limit-match"></a>
If $p_k\in W$ tends to $p$, compactness of $N_1$ and $U(2)$, using the fixed global trivializations, gives a subsequence for which $\Phi(p_k)\to q$ and $J(p_k)\to J_0$. Continuity of every source solution gives $$\label{bdy:limit-match}
                         J_0 I_2(p)=I_1(q).$$ The point $q$ cannot lie on the boundary, where the right side would be zero, since $I_2(p)$ is onto. Two distinct possible limits $q$ contradict joint surjectivity on $N_1$; at a fixed $q$, surjectivity of $I_2(p)$ determines $J_0$. The full limits therefore exist. Also $q\notin E$: otherwise its identity match on $E$ and <a href="#bdy:limit-match" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bdy:limit-match">[bdy:limit-match]</a> would relate the evaluations at the distinct points $q,p$ of $N_2$. Both $p$ and $q$ consequently avoid $\overline{U_0}$.

Choose two source solutions whose columns give an invertible matrix $F_1(q)$. Their corresponding second-side columns give $F_2(p)$, invertible by <a href="#bdy:limit-match" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bdy:limit-match">[bdy:limit-match]</a>. Shrink the charts so both matrices are invertible. In a harmonic frame, $v=F_j^{-1}w$ satisfies an equation with scalar elliptic principal part and no zeroth-order term, since every column of $F_j$ is harmonic. Lemma <a href="#bdy:evaluations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bdy:evaluations">4</a> is invariant under this smooth frame change. It therefore supplies matrices $X_1^l$ with value zero and gradients equal to any chosen real coordinate covectors times $I$. Choose those covectors independently, and let $X_2^l$ be the corresponding source matrices expressed in $F_2$.

Set $x^l=\frac12\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{tr}X_1^l$ and $y^l=\frac12\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{tr}X_2^l$. The first functions form coordinates at $q$. On matched points, the source identity implies $JF_2=F_1\circ\Phi$ and $X_2^l=X_1^l\circ\Phi$. Thus the scalar coordinates and the metric Gram matrices of their differentials agree under the isometry. Passing to $p,q$, their Gram matrices agree and are positive definite. Hence the $y^l$ are coordinates at $p$. Fix a target chart neighborhood $U_1$ on which the $x^l$ are coordinates. The full convergence of partner points permits a source chart neighborhood $U_2$ so small that $\Phi(W\cap U_2)\subset U_1$. Choose a small coordinate ball $\Omega$ about zero contained in both $x(U_1)$ and $y(U_2)$, and restrict the two charts to their inverse images of $\Omega$. For a matched point in the restricted source chart, $x(\Phi(p'))=y(p')\in\Omega$; its partner therefore lies in the restricted target chart. Thus the old map is coordinate identity throughout its domain in these charts. The matrix gradient identity at $q$ now reads $\partial_iX_1^l(0)=\delta_i^lI$; equality on the one-sided region and smoothness give the same identity for $X_2^l$.

Because the harmonic-frame equation has no zeroth-order term, a jet with zero value and gradient is constrained only by $g_1^{ij}(0)H_{ij}=0$. The jet lemma supplies a finite basis of these Hessians and corresponding source pairs. They agree on the known region, so their complete jets agree at the contact point. The same conclusion holds for every smooth coefficient or paired function already identified there.

Finally polar-orthonormalize the original harmonic frames: write $F_j=H_jS_j$, with $H_j$ unitary and $S_j$ positive Hermitian. On the matched set, $JF_2=F_1$ implies $S_2=S_1$ and $JH_2=H_1$. In the unitary frames $H_j$, the old bundle identification is therefore $I$; connection matrices and Green matrices agree there. We continue to write $F_j$ for the harmonic-frame matrices in these unitary frames. A common real linear coordinate change makes the metric at zero Euclidean and the contact hyperplane horizontal. Apply the same linear change to the list of $X_j^l$; this preserves all identities in <a href="#bdy:trace-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bdy:trace-coordinates">[bdy:trace-coordinates]</a>. Reverse the final normal coordinate if necessary to put the known side below its graph. In these coordinates the Hessian constraint is the one in the statement. ◻

</details>

<a id="bdy:dilation"></a>
#### Dilation of the contact data

<a id="bdy:dilation-formulas"></a>
The local analysis uses the preceding charts on a small physical scale and a fixed coordinate range. For $\lambda>0$, set $$\label{bdy:dilation-formulas}
 \begin{aligned}
 g_{j,\lambda}(x)&=g_j(\lambda x),&
 A_{j,\lambda}(x)&=\lambda A_j(\lambda x),\\
 G_{j,\lambda}(x,y)&=\lambda^{n-2}G_j(\lambda x,\lambda y),&
 F_{j,\lambda}(x)&=F_j(\lambda x).
 \end{aligned}$$ Here the metric normalization is $\lambda^{-2}$ times the pullback metric. Thus the pulled-back connection Laplacian is multiplied by $\lambda^2$, and the factor $\lambda^{n-2}$ in the kernel exactly compensates for metric volume. In particular the displayed kernel still has unit matrix point source for $g_{j,\lambda}$. Harmonic functions and the harmonic frames dilate by composition, without a multiplicative factor.

On every fixed bounded coordinate range, these metrics converge smoothly to $I_n$, the connection matrices converge smoothly to zero, and all fixed finite orders of their coefficients and frames remain bounded. Source-free harmonic-frame operators have zero zeroth-order terms; their ordinary-coordinate first-order coefficients tend to zero. The known side becomes $x_n<\lambda^{-1}\kappa(\lambda x')$, which contains every fixed bounded region lying strictly below $x_n=0$ for all sufficiently small $\lambda$.

We also have uniform kernel bounds on fixed separated sets. An interior parametrix of order $-2$ for the original smooth scalar-principal system gives $|G_j(x,y)|\le C|x-y|^{2-n}$ in a sufficiently small fixed chart, with a smooth remainder; interior estimates give the corresponding bounds for derivatives away from the diagonal. After <a href="#bdy:dilation-formulas" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bdy:dilation-formulas">[bdy:dilation-formulas]</a>, every fixed positive lower bound for $|x-y|$ therefore gives bounds of any prescribed finite order, uniformly for small $\lambda$. The local constructions below first specify their bounded ranges and positive separation margins and then restrict $\lambda$. They never demand a bound uniform as the separation tends to zero from this initialization alone.

<a id="bdy:harmonic-normalization"></a>
For the local argument, our convention for a normalized equation in a harmonic frame is $$\label{bdy:harmonic-normalization}
 \mathcal L_jv=-c_jF_j^{-1}L_{g_j,A_j}(F_jv),\qquad c_j>0.$$ The scalar multiplier acts outside the differential operator. Its second-derivative coefficient matrix is $c_jg_j^{-1}$, and its zeroth-order term is zero. This convention applies to both the original and the dilated data; the functions $c_j$ will be chosen in Section <a href="#sec:spheres" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:spheres">4</a>.

The remaining local task is to continue the match through zero. Sections <a href="#sec:product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:product">[sec:product]</a>–<a href="#sec:matrix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:matrix">6</a> will prove that the metrics are conformal and that suitable normalizations <a href="#bdy:harmonic-normalization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bdy:harmonic-normalization">[bdy:harmonic-normalization]</a> give equal operators on a neighborhood of zero. Section <a href="#glob:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="glob:section">7</a> will then use the vanishing zeroth-order terms to remove the conformal factor and prove that $F_1F_2^{-1}$ is a unitary connection identification. Unique continuation extends every source evaluation, so the local identification enlarges the maximal match.

<a id="cross:section"></a>
### Continuation of solutions in two variables

<span id="sec:product" label="sec:product"></span>

The local data give a matrix Green kernel when at least one variable lies in the region where the two structures already agree. We must continue this kernel to separated pairs outside that region. This section proves the required continuation for elliptic systems acting on separate tensor indices. The geometric construction and its scalar analytic foundation are those of (OpenAI 2026, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[ScalarCompanion2026](#ref-ScalarCompanion2026)]; we reproduce the argument, including the interpolation and overlap constructions, and make the extension to systems explicit. No scalar inverse-problem uniqueness theorem is used.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Separate equations and extension from a cross

Let $V_i$ be finite-dimensional Hermitian vector spaces. In a coordinate domain in $\mathbb R^n$, consider an operator $$\mathsf L_i=-\Delta_{g_i}I_{V_i}
                   +C_i^a\partial_a+D_i,$$ where $g_i$ is a smooth Riemannian metric and $C_i^a,D_i$ are smooth endomorphism-valued coefficients. A *product solution* is a $V_1\otimes V_2$-valued function $F(x,y)$ satisfying $$(\mathsf L_1^x\otimes I)F=0,
 \qquad (I\otimes\mathsf L_2^y)F=0.$$ We suppress the identity factors below. The coefficients of one equation act only on its indicated tensor index and depend only on its indicated variable. In particular, the two equations commute. Their sum has scalar real elliptic principal part on the product; ordinary unique continuation therefore identifies product solutions that agree on a nonempty open subset of a connected common domain. Multiplication of either equation by a positive scalar function does not change its solutions or any continuation constructed from them.

For matrix Green kernels the first tensor factor is the output fiber. The second is the conjugate of the input fiber: the equation in $y$ is obtained by conjugating the coefficients of the second connection Laplacian. Equivalently, the adjoint columns of the kernel solve the second equation. This convention places the two matrix actions on separate indices, as required here.

The first construction extends compatible data on $(D_1\times S_2)\cup(S_1\times D_2)$. Its mechanism is a basis that is orthogonal both on a large domain and after restriction to the observed set. Common-basis methods have a classical antecedent in separately harmonic extension (Zériahi 1982) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Zeriahi1982](#ref-Zeriahi1982)]; singular systems also enter quantitative Runge approximation (Rüland and Salo 2019) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[RulandSalo2019](#ref-RulandSalo2019)]. The interpolation threshold and the construction below follow the scalar companion.

<a id="stmt-cross-series"></a>
<div id="cross:series" class="lemma">

<a id="cross:interpolation-input"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Extension from a cross). *Let $D_i$ be bounded connected coordinate domains, and let $S_i\Subset D_i$ be nonempty open sets. Suppose a product solution $F$ is given consistently on $$(D_1\times S_2)\cup(S_1\times D_2),$$ with the sum of its two $L^2$ norms at most $M$. Suppose $O_i\Subset D_i$ are open sets and every $V_i$-valued solution $w$ of $\mathsf L_iw=0$ on $D_i$ satisfies $$\label{cross:interpolation-input}
 \|w\|_{L^2(O_i)}
 \le C\|w\|_{L^2(S_i)}^{\gamma_i}
          \|w\|_{L^2(D_i)}^{1-\gamma_i},
 \qquad 0<\gamma_i<1,\qquad \gamma_1+\gamma_2>1.$$ Then $F$ extends as a product solution to $O_1\times O_2$, agreeing with the two given pieces on their intersections. Its $C^m$ norm on every compact subset of the output product is at most $C_mM$. These constants are uniform when geometric margins, ellipticity, the required coefficient bounds, interpolation constants, and a positive lower bound for $\gamma_1+\gamma_2-1$ are uniform. A specified output order requires only finitely many coefficient bounds.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cross-series"><summary>Proof of stmt-cross-series</summary>

<a id="cross:singular-decay"></a>
*Proof.* Let $\mathcal H_1(D_1)$ be the closed subspace of $L^2(D_1;V_1)$ consisting of distributional $\mathsf L_1$-solutions. Restriction $R:\mathcal H_1(D_1)\to L^2(S_1;V_1)$ is compact by interior elliptic estimates and is injective by unique continuation. The spectral theorem for $R^*R$ gives an orthonormal basis $(w_j)$ of $\mathcal H_1(D_1)$ such that <a id="cross:singular-values"></a>$$\label{cross:singular-values}
 (w_i,w_j)_{L^2(S_1)}=\mu_j^2\delta_{ij},\qquad 0<\mu_j\le1.$$ For every $N$ there is a uniform constant $C_N$ with $$\label{cross:singular-decay}
 \mu_j\le C_N(1+j)^{-N}.$$ Indeed, multiply a solution by a cutoff equal to one near $\overline{S_1}$ and compactly supported in $D_1$. Interior estimates bound its $H^k$ norm by its $L^2(D_1)$ norm. Fourier truncation in a containing cube, component by component, gives an approximation of rank $O(A^n)$ and error $O(A^{-k})$. The minimax characterization of singular values yields $\mu_j\le C_kj^{-k/n}$. The fixed fiber dimension changes only the constants.

For $y\in S_2$, expansion in the first variable gives $$F(x,y)=\sum_j w_j(x)\otimes b_j(y),\qquad
 \|b_j\|_{L^2(S_2;V_2)}\le M.$$ Contracting the first tensor index against $\overline{w_j}$ extends the coefficients to $D_2$: <a id="cross:coefficient-extension"></a>$$\label{cross:coefficient-extension}
 b_j(y)=\mu_j^{-2}\int_{S_1}
                  \sum_\alpha\overline{w_{j,\alpha}(x)}F_{\alpha}(x,y)\,dx,
 \qquad \|b_j\|_{L^2(D_2;V_2)}\le M\mu_j^{-1}.$$ Here $F_\alpha(x,y)\in V_2$ are the first-index components in an orthonormal basis of $V_1$; the displayed contraction leaves a vector in $V_2$. Because the second system acts on that remaining index, $\mathsf L_2b_j=0$. Restriction orthogonality proves that this formula agrees with the original coefficient on $S_2$. The assumed interpolation gives $$\|w_j\|_{L^2(O_1)}\le C\mu_j^{\gamma_1},\qquad
 \|b_j\|_{L^2(O_2)}\le CM\mu_j^{\gamma_2-1}.$$ Thus the tensor series converges absolutely in $L^2(O_1\times O_2)$: the norm of its $j$th term is at most $CM\mu_j^{\gamma_1+\gamma_2-1}$, and <a href="#cross:singular-decay" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cross:singular-decay">[cross:singular-decay]</a> makes these bounds summable. The limit solves both equations distributionally. Interior estimates for their elliptic sum give smoothness and the claimed bounds.

Agreement on the second arm requires a completeness check. Take $z\in L^2(S_1;V_1)$ orthogonal to the closure of $R\mathcal H_1(D_1)$. The $V_2$-valued function $$y\longmapsto\int_{S_1}\sum_\alpha\overline{z_\alpha(x)}F_\alpha(x,y)\,dx$$ solves the second system and vanishes on $S_2$, hence vanishes on connected $D_2$. Every second-arm slice therefore belongs to the closed restricted range. Its expansion in the orthonormal basis $w_j/\mu_j$ is complete, with coefficients $\mu_jb_j(y)$. Consequently the series equals $F$ on $S_1\times O_2$; it converges there by the positive exponent $\gamma_2$. On $O_1\times S_2$ it is the original expansion, converging with exponent $\gamma_1$. These establish both required agreements. Only upper bounds for $\mu_j$ have been used, so uniformity does not require continuous choices of singular bases or lower bounds on singular values. ◻

</details>

The remaining issue is geometric: we must place two observed sets so that the interpolation exponents add to more than one. Strict Carleman weights will supply those exponents from a curvature condition on the boundary of the region where the product solution is known.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### A geometric criterion for local extension

<a id="cross:strict-weight"></a>
<a id="cross:carleman"></a>
We next build the interpolation estimates needed in Lemma <a href="#cross:series" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cross:series">7</a>. For a metric $g$, call a smooth real function $\theta$ a *strict weight* at a point where $d\theta\ne0$ if $$\label{cross:strict-weight}
 \operatorname{Hess}_g\theta(e,e)
  +\operatorname{Hess}_g\theta(v,v)>0
 \quad\text{for all }v\perp e,\ |v|_g=1,
 \qquad e=\frac{\nabla^g\theta}{|\nabla^g\theta|_g}.$$ For the system $\mathsf L=-\Delta_gI+C^a\partial_a+D$, the strict Carleman estimate gives, after shrinking the coordinate neighborhood, $$\label{cross:carleman}
 \tau^3\|e^{\tau\theta}w\|_{L^2}^2
 +\tau\|\nabla(e^{\tau\theta}w)\|_{L^2}^2
 \le C\|e^{\tau\theta}\mathsf Lw\|_{L^2}^2,
 \qquad \tau\ge\tau_0,$$ for compactly supported vector-valued $w$. All norms sum over the fiber components. The scalar form is the elliptic strict-weight estimate of the classical Carleman theory; see (Hörmander 2009, chap. XXVIII) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[HormanderIV2009](#ref-HormanderIV2009)] and (Koch and Tataru 2005, Definition 8.3 and Theorem 9(a)) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[KochTataru2005](#ref-KochTataru2005)]. We recall its energy proof to record the system dependence. First take one scalar component and $\mathsf L=-\Delta_g$, write $v=e^{\tau\theta}w$ and $$e^{\tau\theta}\mathsf Le^{-\tau\theta}=A_\tau+B_\tau,\qquad
 A_\tau=-\Delta_g-\tau^2|d\theta|_g^2,\quad
 B_\tau=\tau(2\nabla^g\theta\cdot\nabla+\Delta_g\theta).$$ Here $A_\tau$ is self-adjoint and $B_\tau$ is skew-adjoint for metric volume. Integration by parts gives $$\begin{aligned}
 \|(A_\tau+B_\tau)v\|^2
 &=\|A_\tau v\|^2+\|B_\tau v\|^2
       +([A_\tau,B_\tau]v,v),\\
 ([A_\tau,B_\tau]v,v)
 &=4\tau\int\operatorname{Hess}_g\theta(\nabla v,\nabla\overline v)
   +4\tau^3\int\operatorname{Hess}_g\theta
                 (\nabla\theta,\nabla\theta)|v|^2
   -\tau\int(\Delta_g^2\theta)|v|^2.
\end{aligned}$$ Choose a real constant $m$ strictly between the negative of the least unit tangential Hessian value and the unit normal Hessian value. After shrinking the patch, these inequalities have a positive margin. Add $4m\tau(A_\tau v,v)$ to the commutator form. Its gradient coefficient becomes $4\tau(\operatorname{Hess}_g\theta+mg)$, and its leading zero-order coefficient is $$4\tau^3|d\theta|_g^2
       \big(\operatorname{Hess}_g\theta(e,e)-m\big)>c\tau^3.$$ The tangential gradient form is positive. Its mixed and possibly negative normal terms cost at most $C\tau\|\nabla_e v\|^2$, and $$\|\nabla_e v\|^2
 \le C\tau^{-2}\|B_\tau v\|^2+C\|v\|^2.$$ Finally $|4m\tau(A_\tau v,v)|\le\tfrac12\|A_\tau v\|^2+
C\tau^2\|v\|^2$. For large $\tau$, the squared parts absorb the normal derivative cost and the positive zero-order term absorbs the remaining errors. This yields the scalar estimate. Summing it over components gives the estimate for $-\Delta_gI$. The conjugated matrix lower-order terms have norm at most $C(\|\nabla v\|+\tau\|v\|)$, so their squared norm is absorbed by the $\tau$ and $\tau^3$ terms after increasing $\tau_0$. This proves <a href="#cross:carleman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cross:carleman">[cross:carleman]</a> for the stated systems. The constants are uniform on compact sets of strict weights, with fixed positive strictness margins and bounded matrix coefficients. The scalar cutoff commutators used below act componentwise, and interior estimates for these systems have the same orders as in the scalar case.

The existence criterion below is the geometric criterion of (OpenAI 2026, sec. 3, “A geometric criterion for local extension”) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[ScalarCompanion2026](#ref-ScalarCompanion2026)]. Its geometric proof depends only on the principal metrics. The system estimate just proved and Lemma <a href="#cross:series" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cross:series">7</a> provide the analytic steps in the present setting. All gradients, lengths, orthogonality relations, and Hessians in its statement use the principal metrics $g_1,g_2$ and their product connection.

<a id="stmt-cross-criterion"></a>
<div id="cross:criterion" class="proposition">

<a id="cross:hessian-test"></a>
**Proposition 8** (Product extension criterion). *Let $\rho$ be smooth near $z_0=(x_0,y_0)$, with $\rho(z_0)=0$ and $d_x\rho,d_y\rho\ne0$. Put $$a_i=\frac{\nabla_i\rho}{|\nabla_i\rho|^2},\qquad H=\operatorname{Hess}\rho.$$ Suppose that at $z_0$ $$\label{cross:hessian-test}
 H((a_1,-a_2),(a_1,-a_2))
 +H((v_1,v_2),(v_1,v_2))>0
 \quad
 \left(v_i\perp a_i,\ |v_i|=|a_i|\right).$$ Every product solution given on $\{\rho>0\}$ near $z_0$ extends to a neighborhood of $z_0$, agreeing on a smaller part of that side. In fact, the construction uses only two compact product sets in $\{\rho>\varepsilon\}$ for some $\varepsilon>0$. The neighborhoods and estimates on smaller neighborhoods are uniform under small smooth perturbations preserving the strict hypotheses, provided the data on those compact sets are bounded.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cross-criterion"><summary>Proof of stmt-cross-criterion</summary>
